Rests on: [Q-Align and Q-ReAlign], [DINOv2 weights and licence], [IQA-PyTorch (pyiqa) toolbox and NR benchmark].
- Do not compute Inception FID, and do not compute any Frechet distance on a handful of renders: the covariance has 2048 (Inception), 1024 (DINOv2-L) or 768 (DINOv2-B) dimensions, FID is biased in a model-dependent way, and published guidance is 10k+ samples. Rests on: [Effectively Unbiased FID and Inception Score (FID bias)], [Exposing flaws of generative model evaluation metrics (FD-DINOv2 paper)], [Rethinking FID: Towards a Better Evaluation Metric for Image Generation (CMMD paper)].
- Primary distribution metric: an UNBIASED MMD^2 (drop diagonal terms) between patch embeddings of the probe renders and a fixed real reference bank, using CLIP ViT-L/14@336 (CMMD settings: RBF sigma=10, x1000 scale, so numbers stay comparable to the literature) and, as a second opinion, DINOv2-B/14. The public CMMD code is the biased estimator and, with sigma=10 on unit-norm embeddings, behaves like 10*||mean difference||^2 whose small-n noise floor (about 10*(trSigma/n + trSigma/m)) can exceed real model-to-model gaps. Rests on: [CMMD reference implementations (google-research JAX and cmmd-pytorch)], [FID/KID tooling: clean-fid, torch-fidelity, piq], [dgm-eval library (FD-DINOv2, KD, Realism, Density/Coverage)].
- Sample plan (our recommendation, unmeasured): 4-8 probe frames x 2 cameras per round at fixed poses/times/seeds; 32-64 random crops per frame at native sensor scale (224 px, and 336 px for CLIP), stratified by region (sky/horizon/near ground/trees/buildings/road) -> 256-512 patches; real bank of at least 2,000 patches embedded once and cached per encoder version. Patches from one frame are correlated, so report a cluster bootstrap that resamples FRAMES, not patches. Rests on: [CMMD reference implementations (google-research JAX and cmmd-pytorch)], [Feature Likelihood Divergence: KID stays consistent at small test sizes].
- Calibrate before trusting any number, and again whenever encoder, bank or code changes: (i) real-vs-real split (same n) gives the noise floor; (ii) the last accepted render gives the regression reference; (iii) deliberately degraded real crops (blur, noise, desaturation, haze removal, exposure shift) give sensitivity checks; (iv) a SkyScenes sample gives a known-gap synthetic anchor. Accept an edit only if the primary metric improves by more than 2x the real-vs-real spread AND no guard metric regresses. Rests on: [Camera simulation for robot simulation: which camera-model components matter], [SkyScenes (CARLA aerial synthetic dataset)], [SEDD style-based sim-to-real gap metric].
- Minimal Tier A set (numpy/PIL/scipy only, per frame, seconds): A1 radially averaged luminance power spectrum - log-log slope in a mid band and exponential tail decay of the high frequencies - catches over-smooth or over-sharpened textures, resampling and missing/excess noise, blind to phase and layout; A2 MSCN/gradient statistics (generalised-Gaussian shape, kurtosis, patch features with an MVG refit on the REAL aerial bank = 'aerial NIQE') catches CG-clean local contrast and banding; A3 Lab/Oklab colour and luminance statistics per region (sky, vegetation, road, roof): mean, covariance, saturation percentiles, sky/ground luminance ratio - catches albedo, exposure and white-balance errors, which dominate sim-to-real difference; A4 depth-binned haze curve (see the aerial-perspective rule). Compare to reference statistics per scene type and orientation, not to universal constants. Rests on: [Natural-image power-spectrum statistics], [Fourier Spectrum Discrepancies in Deep Network Generated Images], [A Comprehensive Exploration of Fidelity Quantification in Computer-Generated Images (Sensors 2024)], [NIQE and BRISQUE (background knowledge, not verified this session)], [Camera simulation for robot simulation: which camera-model components matter].
- Minimal Tier B set (torch): B1 the unbiased CLIP and DINOv2 patch MMD above (catches global look and appearance mismatch); B2 per-patch k-nearest-neighbour distance to the real bank in DINOv2 space (dgm-eval Realism/Density style), shown as a heat map over the frame (catches LOCAL fake-looking regions; works with one image because the bank, not the probe set, carries the sample size); B3 LPIPS(alex) and DreamSim between last accepted and candidate renders of the same frame (change-magnitude and unintended-regression guard, not realism); B4 an IQA drop detector (MUSIQ or CLIP-IQA+ or TOPIQ-NR, optional Q-Align 4/8-bit) flagging blur, noise or exposure regressions only. Rests on: [dgm-eval library (FD-DINOv2, KD, Realism, Density/Coverage)], [LPIPS (Learned Perceptual Image Patch Similarity)], [DreamSim], [IQA-PyTorch (pyiqa) toolbox and NR benchmark], [Q-Align and Q-ReAlign].
- Never put an IQA score (NIQE, BRISQUE, MUSIQ, CLIP-IQA, Q-Align, DeQA-Score, Q-Insight, VisualQuality-R1) in the accept criterion: they are trained on camera-photo MOS (KonIQ-10k, SPAQ, LIVEC), NIQE/BRISQUE correlate weakly even there (KonIQ SRCC 0.377 and 0.230), and the SEDD paper argues NR-IQA can overrate clean but unrealistic synthetic images. Use them to flag a drop beyond 1 std of the last accepted render. Rests on: [IQA-PyTorch (pyiqa) toolbox and NR benchmark], [SEDD style-based sim-to-real gap metric], [Q-Insight family and VisualQuality-R1 (reasoning IQA, 2025-2026)], [DeQA-Score], [CLIP-IQA].
- Aerial-perspective check (the 2 km, 40 m flight makes it central): with the Replicator depth AOV bin pixels by distance (for example 0-50, 50-200, 200-500, 500-1000, 1000-2000 m) and report per bin the mean dark channel, RMS contrast and mean colour shift toward the sky colour; fit t(d)=exp(-beta d). Expected values are priors, not facts: V=3.912/beta, so V=20 km -> t(2 km) about 0.68 and V=40 km -> about 0.82 (our arithmetic); choose V from real weather/visibility data for the render date. For real footage without depth use FADE and the flat-ground row-to-distance mapping d=h/tan(angle below horizon). Rests on: [Dark channel prior and atmospheric scattering model], [FADE fog density predictor].
- Reference bank, in order of value: (1) 5-20 minutes of the owner's own LUCID-camera footage flown over the real Ames route at 40 m - same camera, place and season, legally clean, a strong recommendation because no public set matches an oblique 40 m view of west Ames; (2) AeroScapes (CC-BY-SA-4.0, 5-50 m) for oblique low-altitude appearance; (3) VDD 30-degree frames and UDD/UAVScenes for research-only appearance statistics (non-commercial; do not redistribute); (4) NAIP nadir tiles for ground colour, field/roof/canopy layout; (5) Mapillary only for ground-level materials, subject to licence confirmation. Keep all real imagery under ~/.cache/drone_scene/reference/ with a manifest (source, licence, date, credit line), never in git. Rests on: [AeroScapes], [VDD, UDD and UAVScenes (non-commercial drone sets)], [VisDrone, Okutama-Action and UAVid (licence not stated in README)], [NAIP on Microsoft Planetary Computer], [Mapillary (street-level imagery, API v4)].
- Do not mix content mismatch into the metric: embeddings encode what is in the picture, so embedding distances against foreign-scene sets (Chinese cities, Japanese fields) mostly measure content difference. Use content-agnostic appearance statistics (Tier A, style/Gram statistics) against foreign references, and MMD/kNN embedding distances only against Midwest/Ames-like references (own footage, NAIP-derived, AeroScapes crops filtered to vegetation/road/roof regions). Rests on: [Scalable Modular Synthetic Data Generation for Advancing Aerial Autonomy], [Quantifying the Synthetic and Real Domain Gap in Aerial Scene Understanding], [SEDD style-based sim-to-real gap metric].
- NAIP nadir cross-check: render an orthographic (or high-altitude nadir) probe of the same footprint at NAIP ground resolution, downsample by area averaging, and compare per-land-cover colour/texture statistics, canopy fraction and roof/field layout - not pixels, because of date/sun/crop differences and +/-4 m geolocation error. With the default 87-degree, 2048-px camera the nadir pixel is about 3.7 cm at 40 m, and a 0.6 m GSD needs about 650 m altitude (our arithmetic from the repo README lens figures); NAIP therefore cannot validate fine texture. Fetch via Planetary Computer STAC (collection 'naip', newest item for bbox [-93.72,42.00,-93.64,42.06], windowed rasterio read); do not hard-code the year or GSD, print them from the items. Credit: USDA Farm Production and Conservation - Business Center, Geospatial Enterprise Operations. Rests on: [NAIP on Microsoft Planetary Computer], [NAIP Planetary Computer access recipe], [NAIP coverage for Iowa], [NAIP on AWS Open Data].
- Fix global colour/exposure/tone first, then textures: each round, log the scene mean radiance versus an 18% grey card, per-region Lab statistics and sky/ground luminance ratio, and prioritise edits that move those toward the real bank before touching micro-detail, lens distortion or noise. Rests on: [Camera simulation for robot simulation: which camera-model components matter], [Natural-image power-spectrum statistics].
- VLM/LLM realism judge protocol (Tier C): blinded pairwise A/B only, each pair judged in both orders and at least 3 samples, accepted only when order-swapped votes agree; put a real photo in about a third of the pools as an anchor and use the judge's acceptance rate on real photos as the achievable ceiling; histogram-match brightness/contrast of the two images in a pair so polish cannot win; require the judge to list image-grounded, locatable artefacts (shadow direction vs sun azimuth, tree/building scale, road width, horizon haze) which a cheaper agent then checks against Tier A numbers or scene data; never use absolute 1-10 realism scores as an objective; calibrate each judge model on (real vs previous render vs deliberately degraded render) and log judge model, prompt hash and version. Rests on: [VLM Judges Can Rank but Cannot Score], [Fooling the LVLM Judges: Visual Biases in LVLM-Based Evaluation (FRAME)], [VLM informativeness bias (ACL 2026)], [LOKI: Synthetic Data Detection Benchmark using Large Multimodal Models], [Scalable Evaluation of the Realism of Synthetic Environmental Augmentations in Images].
- Model tiering: cheap models run metric scripts, tile crops, build tables, fetch reference data and apply mechanical edits in worktrees; mid-tier models write the pairwise critiques and propose material/lighting edits (several cheap-model votes substitute for one expensive absolute score, since absolute VLM scores are unreliable); the strongest model is reserved for adjudicating when metrics and judge disagree, for choosing among conflicting parallel worktree results and for the end-of-session summary. This is a design choice supported only indirectly by the judge-reliability literature. Rests on: [VLM Judges Can Rank but Cannot Score], [LOKI: Synthetic Data Detection Benchmark using Large Multimodal Models].
- Parallelism boundary: code/material/lighting edits can be prepared in parallel git worktrees by cheap agents, but ONE GPU queue serialises renders and metric runs; each candidate renders the identical probe set (same poses, times, seeds, sensor-noise seed) so LPIPS/DreamSim and the metric deltas are paired comparisons; keep only candidates that pass the accept rule, merge one at a time, then re-render the merged result to verify it before the next merge. Rests on: [LPIPS (Learned Perceptual Image Patch Similarity)], [DreamSim], [Effectively Unbiased FID and Inception Score (FID bias)].
- Use a real-vs-sim patch discriminator only as a diagnostic: a logistic regression or linear probe on frozen DINOv2 patch features (or spectral-tail features) trained on real bank vs renders, whose per-patch logit map points the agents to the most fake-looking regions; freeze it for several rounds and re-fit on fresh renders, because optimising directly against a fixed detector is gameable (spectral synthesis attacks). Rests on: [Fourier Spectrum Discrepancies in Deep Network Generated Images], [Enhancing Photorealism Enhancement], [dgm-eval library (FD-DINOv2, KD, Realism, Density/Coverage)].
- Licence hygiene for the repo: core metrics in numpy/torch plus permissive packages only - lpips (BSD), dreamsim (MIT), piq (Apache-2.0), torch-fidelity (Apache-2.0), clean-fid (MIT), dgm-eval (MIT), cmmd-pytorch (Apache-2.0), DINOv2 (Apache-2.0). Keep pyiqa (PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0 plus NTU S-Lab terms) and Q-Align/DeQA (S-Lab non-commercial / unstated) outside the repo as optional pip installs in the metric venv; they are fine for the owner's private non-commercial research but must not be vendored or redistributed. Never pip-install any of this into the Isaac Sim Python environment. Rests on: [FID/KID tooling: clean-fid, torch-fidelity, piq], [LPIPS (Learned Perceptual Image Patch Similarity)], [DreamSim], [IQA-PyTorch (pyiqa) toolbox and NR benchmark], [Q-Align and Q-ReAlign], [DeQA-Score], [DINOv2 weights and licence].
- Log every probe round as one JSON line (render commit, config hash, camera, pose set, encoder and bank versions, every Tier A/B number with bootstrap CI, judge verdicts, accept/reject reason) next to the existing drone_scene log, and cache embeddings of the real bank per encoder version so reference data is embedded once. Keep the owner as the final visual gate: published work finds no automatic metric strongly tracks human-perceived realism. Rests on: [Exposing flaws of generative model evaluation metrics (FD-DINOv2 paper)], [Quantifying the Synthetic and Real Domain Gap in Aerial Scene Understanding].

### Rules the fact-checker says to drop or fix

- Licence hygiene rule: FIX. It says DeQA is 'unstated', but the DeQA-Score repo is MIT. It is built on Q-Align / mPLUG-Owl2, so check downstream terms. Q-Insight and VisualQuality-R1 are Apache-2.0 (the report says 'no licence'). Add that clean-fid bundles an Inception torchscript model under NVIDIA's Source Code License, so 'clean-fid (MIT)' is not the whole picture. Add that the original CLIP-IQA repo is S-Lab non-commercial (piq's implementation is Apache-2.0). Add that dgm-eval, DreamSim and similar tools download backbone weights with their own licences.
- Do not compute Inception FID / no Frechet on a handful of renders: KEEP the core, which is the rank-deficient covariance argument (n below the feature dimension: 2048 / 1024 / 768), but FIX the support. 'Published guidance is 10k+ samples' and the Chong and Forsyth statement (arXiv unopened, 5k-still-biased claim of unknown origin) are unverified. State the maths instead, or label the 10k as unconfirmed.
- Primary metric rule (unbiased CLIP/DINOv2 MMD): FIX. The sigma=10 / x1000 / 'comparable with the literature' reasoning is valid only for L2-normalised CLIP ViT-L/14@336 embeddings as in cmmd-pytorch. Reusing sigma=10 on DINOv2-B/14 features (not unit-norm by default) breaks both the comparability and the first-order '10*||dmu||^2' relation. The 'noise floor can exceed model gaps' point depends on the assumed, unmeasured tr(Sigma)=0.3; present it as a hypothesis. The CMMD paper's 'unbiased' claim was not read. The repo docstring says biased and unbiased are 'almost identical'.
- Sample plan rule: FIX the support. The FLD / 'KID consistent at small n' evidence is a search summary under a made-up heading, not the FLD paper title, and was not read. The numbers (4-8 frames, 32-64 crops, 2,000-patch bank) are unmeasured recommendations, as the report says.
- Calibration rule: DROP the three citations as support (Elmquist, SkyScenes, SEDD are all unopened, and the Elmquist title looks paraphrased). The procedure (real-vs-real split, degraded-real sensitivity, last-accepted regression) is sound methodology on its own. The '2x real-vs-real spread' threshold is an arbitrary choice, not sourced.
- Tier A rule: FIX. The Natural-image spectrum paper, the Sensors 2024 paper and the NIQE/BRISQUE originals are all unverified. The Dzanic 99.2% / 8-example figures are unverified, and the Chandrasegaran follow-up should be cited as 'minor change in the last upsampling operation', not 'final scaling step'. The 'colour/exposure/WB dominate sim-to-real difference' premise rests only on the unopened Elmquist paper.
- IQA-score rule: FIX the wording. Not all of NIQE, BRISQUE, MUSIQ, CLIP-IQA, Q-Align, DeQA, Q-Insight and VisualQuality-R1 are 'trained on camera-photo MOS (KonIQ-10k, SPAQ, LIVEC)'. In the pyiqa protocol NR models are trained on KonIQ-10k and tested on LIVEC, SPAQ and others; NIQE is opinion-unaware (per the report's own description); several VLM scorers use other mixes (DeQA: KonIQ, SPAQ, KADID). The weak KonIQ correlations (NIQE 0.377, BRISQUE 0.230) are verified. The SEDD support is unverified.
- Aerial-perspective rule: KEEP, but note the sources are unopened. The dark channel prior / FADE papers could not be reached. The beta and t(2 km) arithmetic is verified (0.676 and 0.822). Treat the chosen visibility V as an input to be sourced.
- Content-mismatch rule: KEEP the principle but drop the three citations as evidence (Scalable Modular Synthetic Data, Marcu, SEDD are all unopened).
- NAIP cross-check rule: FIX the credit line and accuracy figure. 'USDA Farm Production and Conservation - Business Center, Geospatial Enterprise Operations' and the '+/-4 m' accuracy come from unopened Earth Engine / FSA pages. Take the credit text and accuracy from the STAC item or FGDC metadata the script downloads. The GSD arithmetic (3.7 cm at 40 m, ~648 m for 0.6 m) and the AWS / Planetary Computer facts are verified, and the rule's no-hard-coding stance is right.
- 'Fix global colour/exposure/tone first' rule: DROP the citations as evidence (Elmquist and the natural-image spectrum paper are unopened). Keep it as a plain engineering heuristic.
- VLM judge protocol rule: FIX the support. Only the 'rank but cannot score' paper is partly corroborated (its README says widths vary up to 70% across 14 categories, not the report's 40% vs 70%). FRAME, the ACL 2026 informativeness paper and the Advai paper are all unopened. The LOKI numbers are unreconciled. Keep the protocol as design (order swaps, real anchors, histogram matching) but do not cite the 40% / 70%, 17%, 3.6x or GPT-4o figures.
- Patch-discriminator rule: KEEP. The 'fixed detector is gameable' point is supported by the Chandrasegaran follow-up (CVPR 2021 Oral). Do not rely on the EPE repo for method details (README has none; repo is archived). Dzanic details are unverified.
- Logging rule: FIX the closing sentence. 'Published work finds no automatic metric strongly tracks human-perceived realism' rests on the FD-DINOv2 paper abstract, which was not read here. The dgm-eval README only supports the DINOv2 recommendation.
- Reference bank rule: KEEP, with two caveats. 'No public set matches an oblique 40 m view of west Ames' is an unverified negative. Mapillary's CC BY-SA and token details are unconfirmed, as the rule already says 'subject to licence confirmation'.
- GPU scheduling, model tiering and parallelism rules: KEEP as design choices. Their only factual hooks (Q-Align being a ~7B mPLUG-Owl2 model, pyiqa / DINOv2 facts) check out. They should not be described as literature-backed beyond that.

### Open questions

- Iowa NAIP specifics are unconfirmed: which years exist for the west-Ames bbox on Planetary Computer (2019/2021/2023/2025?), what ground sample distance each year has (0.3 vs 0.6 m), and whether the live STAC extent goes past the 2023-12-31 in the repo template. One STAC query on the owner PC (print item.datetime and item.properties['gsd'] for bbox [-93.72,42.00,-93.64,42.06]) settles it.
- Mapillary licence (believed CC BY-SA 4.0), API v4 token procedure and rate limits are unconfirmed because mapillary.com and mapillary.github.io did not resolve and the WebSearch budget (200 calls per turn) ran out; same for UAVid (licence, altitude, pitch), VisDrone and Okutama-Action terms, and Iowa State GIS Facility terms of use.
- Tool access: WebFetch resolved only github.com, raw.githubusercontent.com and pypi.org. arxiv.org, openreview.net, CVF, NeurIPS/ICML proceedings, Semantic Scholar, HuggingFace and most project pages gave ENOTFOUND, so every paper-level claim here (all findings marked false for primary source) comes from search-result abstracts or summaries, not the paper body. Exact figures to re-check: CMMD Fig. 8 sample-size curves, LOKI per-model accuracy (summaries conflict: 63.9% vs 80.8% for GPT-4o), Elmquist per-component sim-to-real effects, DeQA-Score and SEDD tables.
- No study was found that measures multimodal-LLM reliability at judging rendered-vs-real PHOTOREALISM of aerial/drone imagery; the closest are LOKI (general synthetic detection), the rank-vs-score and FRAME judge-bias papers, and the Advai VLM-jury paper (no human validation). The fleet should measure its own judge's accuracy on real vs rendered vs degraded images before using it.
- Not searched before the budget ended: original MUSIQ, NIQE, BRISQUE and KID (Binkowski 2018) papers; drone Gaussian-splatting or photogrammetry sims with measured appearance gaps; other real low-altitude oblique drone video sets with open licences (for example OpenDroneMap or Wikimedia Commons footage, ICG/Semantic Drone, UAV123, DroneVehicle); recent distribution metrics such as APEX (arXiv 2605.07786, title seen only) and normalizing-flow metrics (arXiv 2410.02004).
- Whether CLIP-L/14 and DINOv2 embeddings are sensitive enough to aerial texture, colour and haze defects at 224-336 px crops, and what the real values of tr(Sigma) and the real-vs-real noise floor are, can only be answered empirically on the owner PC with the calibration rule (real-vs-real split, degraded-real tests, SkyScenes anchor).
- The user-level goal (get the fleet agents into the seyedalicheraghi/IsaacSim repo) is outside this research step; nothing was written, pushed or run here. Pushing still requires the owner's approval per CLAUDE.md.

### Fact-checker summary

I checked 43 findings. Only github.com, raw.githubusercontent.com and pypi.org resolved. arxiv.org, openreview, CVF, NeurIPS, ACL, Semantic Scholar, HuggingFace, NCBI, colorado.edu, CUHK, geoplatform.gov, gis.iastate.edu and hoffman-lab.github.io all failed with ENOTFOUND. The WebSearch budget was already used up, so I made no search lookups. Every claim that depends on a paper body, abstract or those hosts is 'unverifiable', and source_exists=false means 'could not confirm', not 'does not exist'. I ran no code and installed nothing.

Fully supported (13):
- CMMD reference code and port: sigma 10, x1000, biased docstring, 7.696, COCO-30k table.
- dgm-eval.
- torch-fidelity, clean-fid and piq.
- LPIPS.
- DreamSim.
- pyiqa: the licence and all quoted benchmark numbers match tests/NR_benchmark_results.csv.
- Q-Align.
- Both NAIP / Planetary Computer entries, including the arithmetic.
- NAIP on AWS, with the layout details coming from awslabs/open-data-docs, not the yaml the report cites.
- Mapillary SDK.
- AeroScapes.
- VDD, UDD and UAVScenes.
- VisDrone, Okutama and UAVid.
- DINOv2.

Verified wrong in the report:
- DeQA-Score has a licence: the repo is MIT, not 'no licence stated'.
- Q-Insight and VisualQuality-R1 are Apache-2.0, not 'no licence'.
- Dzanic follow-up: it is 'last upsampling operation', not 'final scaling step'.
- 'Feature Likelihood Divergence: KID stays consistent...' is not a paper title.
- The FD-DINOv2 title is truncated.
- The Elmquist title looks paraphrased.
- The VLM-judge '~40% vs ~70% of score range' reading is not in the README (it says 'up to 70%').
- LOKI model counts and accuracies are not on the README.
- clean-fid's bundled Inception model has an NVIDIA licence the report omits.

Own arithmetic that checks out: CMMD first-order 10*||dmu||^2 and the 0.376 / 0.013 floors (given the assumed tr(Sigma)=0.3), the 3.7 cm and 648 m NAIP figures (the camera numbers match this repo's drone_scene README), and the visibility transmissions.

Design rules to fix or drop are in the second list. The most important are the licence-hygiene rule, the sigma=10 transfer to DINOv2, the IQA 'trained on MOS' wording, and the many citations that rest on unopened arXiv papers.

Separately, the user-level goal (get the agents into seyedalicheraghi/IsaacSim) is outside this fact-check step. I wrote and pushed nothing, and pushing needs the owner's approval per CLAUDE.md.

## claude-code-features

### Custom subagents: file locations and complete frontmatter

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched; features cite Claude Code v2.1.2xx, newest v2.1.296), Claude Code docs, sub-agents page (fetched in full except last ~11k chars))
- **Claim:** A subagent is a Markdown file: YAML frontmatter + body (body = its system prompt). Only name and description are required. Field names are camelCase and must match exactly; unknown fields are silently ignored. FULL FIELD LIST (docs table): name (unique, <=256 chars, no ':' , must not start with '-'; hooks receive it as agent_type), description (when to delegate), tools (comma string or YAML list; if omitted inherits every tool available to subagents), disallowedTools (removed from the inherited/listed set; wins if a tool is in both), model (sonnet | opus | haiku | fable | full model ID such as claude-opus-5-5 or claude-sonnet-5 | inherit), permissionMode (default | acceptEdits | auto | dontAsk | bypassPermissions | plan | manual[=default]), maxTurns (result returned marked partial; Claude can resume it), autoCompactWindow (plain integer 100000-1000000, v2.1.296+), skills (full skill content preloaded; first 32 names), mcpServers (name reference or inline server definition), hooks (scoped to this subagent), memory (user | project | local), background (true = always background), omitClaudeMd (true = skip CLAUDE.md files, v2.1.271+), effort (low | medium | high | xhigh | max, model-dependent), isolation (worktree), color (red | blue | green | yellow | purple | orange | pink | cyan), initialPrompt (auto-submitted first user turn, only when the agent runs as the main session agent), experimental.cacheTtl (5m | 1h, v2.1.248+). Plugin-shipped subagents ignore permissionMode, mcpServers, hooks, initialPrompt. LOCATIONS/PRIORITY: managed settings (1) > --agents CLI JSON (2, this session only) > .claude/agents/ (3, project, commit to git) > ~/.claude/agents/ (4) > plugin agents/ (5). Scanned recursively; identity comes only from the name field. Edits/new files are picked up within seconds with no restart, EXCEPT: the first file in an agents directory that did not exist at session start, files under --add-dir directories, and sessions started with --disable-slash-commands. Persistent memory dirs: user ~/.claude/agent-memory/<name>/, project .claude/agent-memory/<name>/ (committable; docs recommend project scope), local .claude/agent-memory-local/<name>/; first 200 lines/25KB of MEMORY.md are injected; Read/Write/Edit are auto-enabled when memory is set. Each non-fork subagent starts with a fresh context: its own system prompt + environment details (NOT the Claude Code system prompt), the delegation prompt, CLAUDE.md files (all levels), git status snapshot, preloaded skills, a sibling roster; it does not see the parent conversation.

MINIMAL WORKING EXAMPLE (verbatim shape of the docs' code-reviewer example, adapted; file .claude/agents/realism-critic.md; composed from documented fields, not executed):
---
name: realism-critic
description: Looks at rendered probe frames (PNG paths given in the prompt) and returns a ranked list of realism defects. Use proactively after every probe render.
tools: Read, Grep, Glob
model: sonnet
effort: medium
maxTurns: 12
color: purple
---
You judge photorealism of drone-scene frames. Read every PNG path you are given with the Read tool, then answer with: defect, where in the frame, suspected cause (material/lighting/sensor), confidence.

Other verbatim docs examples: disallowedTools: Write, Edit ; disallowedTools: mcp__github (blocks one MCP server) ; skills: [api-conventions, error-handling-patterns] ; memory: project.
- **Numbers:** name <=256 chars; combined subagent descriptions >15,000 tokens triggers a startup warning; skills preload cap 32 names; MEMORY.md injection 200 lines or 25KB; autoCompactWindow 100000-1000000; maxTurns has no default limit stated
- **For the fleet:** The whole fleet can live as plain .md files under .claude/agents/ in the IsaacSim repo and arrives on the owner's PC with git pull (project scope, priority 3, picked up live). Per-agent model/effort/tools/maxTurns/isolation give the cheap-vs-strong split directly in the file. Because a subagent sees none of the parent conversation, every agent prompt must be self-contained (paths, command, output format).
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: partly
- **Correction:** Checked against https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents. Every field, value list, version number and limit I checked matches. That covers name <=256 chars with no ':' or leading '-', the model, permissionMode, effort and color values, autoCompactWindow 100000-1000000 (v2.1.296+), omitClaudeMd (v2.1.271+), experimental.cacheTtl (v2.1.248+), the 5-level priority, the 15,000-token description warning, the 32 preloaded skills, the 200 lines/25KB MEMORY.md, the 3 memory directories, the plugin-ignored fields, and the three restart exceptions. Two small errors. (1) 'memory: project' is not a verbatim docs example: the docs example is 'memory: user', and 'project' is only the recommended default. (2) The 'refactorer' isolation: worktree example is on the worktrees page, not the sub-agents page. The final 11k characters of the page hold only example subagents.

### Subagent model selection, aliases, effort and cost controls

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs: sub-agents#choose-a-model, model-config, cli-reference)
- **Claim:** MODEL RESOLUTION ORDER for a subagent (docs): 1) per-invocation model parameter, 2) the definition's model frontmatter (inherit = main conversation's model), 3) CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL env var (alias or ID), 4) the main conversation's model. CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL_FORCE=1 (v2.1.257+) forces one model onto every subagent (settings.json env example: {"env":{"CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL":"haiku","CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL_FORCE":"1"}}). CURRENT ALIASES (model-config): default (clears override), best (= fable where available, else opus), fable, sonnet, opus, haiku, sonnet[1m], opus[1m], opusplan (opus in plan mode, sonnet to execute). On the Anthropic API: opus -> Opus 5.5, sonnet -> Sonnet 5.5, haiku -> Haiku 5.5; fable -> Fable 5.1 (needs Fable access on the account; on some plans Fable bills to usage credits with a one-time consent). Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry resolve older versions (e.g. sonnet -> 4.5). Full IDs named in docs: claude-opus-5-5, claude-haiku-5-5, claude-fable-5-1, claude-fable-5, claude-sonnet-5, claude-opus-4-6, claude-sonnet-4-6. (claude-sonnet-5-5 is the ID of the current session model but is not printed in the pages read - unconfirmed in docs.) Aliases move over time; pin with a full ID or ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_<FAMILY>_MODEL. EFFORT: low | medium | high | xhigh | max on Fable 5/5.1, Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5, Opus 5, Sonnet 5, Opus 4.8/4.7; Opus 4.6/Sonnet 4.6 lack xhigh (falls back to high). Default is high, except Opus 5.5/Sonnet 5.5/Haiku 5.5 default to medium. Frontmatter effort overrides the session level but not CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL. A built-in subagent can be re-pointed by defining your own agent with the same name (e.g. a user/project agent named Explore with model: haiku). Built-ins: Explore (read-only; uses main model, or the opus alias target when main is Fable), Plan (inherits), general-purpose (CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL if set, else main). Org allowlist (availableModels) can silently substitute a blocked model.

MINIMAL EXAMPLES: frontmatter `model: haiku` / `model: sonnet` / `model: inherit` / `model: claude-opus-5-5`; whole-run override `claude --model opus --fallback-model sonnet,haiku`.
- **Numbers:** effort levels 5 (low..max); default effort medium on 5.5-generation models, high elsewhere; fallback chain capped at 3 models
- **For the fleet:** Cheap/strong tiering is a per-file decision: put `model: haiku` on log-reading/render-runner agents, `model: sonnet` on critics and code editors, and reserve opus (or fable if the owner has access) for the lead/final-judge. Do not set CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL_FORCE unless you want to flatten the tiers. Pin full model IDs in unattended loops so alias drift does not change cost/behaviour mid-experiment.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: partly
- **Correction:** Checked against model-config, sub-agents and cli-reference. Resolution order, CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL_FORCE (v2.1.257+), the aliases (opus->Opus 5.5, sonnet->Sonnet 5.5, haiku->Haiku 5.5, fable->Fable 5.1), the full IDs, the 3-model fallback cap, and 'frontmatter effort overrides session but not CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL' are all confirmed. Overriding a built-in by defining an agent with the same name (Explore, model: haiku) is confirmed. Bedrock/Vertex resolve sonnet to Sonnet 4.5 (confirmed). Corrections: (1) Opus 4.7 defaults to xhigh, so 'default high except the 5.5 models' is slightly wrong. (2) Explore runs on the opus alias target when main is Fable only for subscription, Console or LLM-gateway sessions. On Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry it stays on the main model. (3) The combined command 'claude --model opus --fallback-model sonnet,haiku' is not printed verbatim, but both flags exist separately. (4) 'claude-sonnet-5-5' is not printed in the part of model-config I read (first 100k of 119k chars). The pages print 'claude-sonnet-5', which is Sonnet 5, not 5.5. The report already marks this unconfirmed. This session's own model ID is claude-sonnet-5-5, which supports it but is not a docs confirmation.

### Automatic delegation, explicit invocation, nesting and Agent(...) allowlist

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs, sub-agents page)
- **Claim:** DELEGATION: Claude delegates based on the user's request, the subagent description fields and current context; to encourage proactive delegation put phrases like 'use proactively' in description. Combined descriptions of non-built-in subagents above 15,000 tokens give a startup warning (agents still load); move detail into the body, which only loads when the agent runs. EXPLICIT INVOCATION: natural language ('Use the realism-critic subagent on these frames') or @-mention typeahead (@"realism-critic (agent)" ...), which guarantees that agent is chosen; Claude still writes the task prompt. CAN A SUBAGENT SPAWN SUBAGENTS? Yes by default, up to three layers below the main conversation; at the depth limit the Agent tool is withheld (except in forks). Change with CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH (settings.json env; 1 turns nesting off, 2 = one extra layer). To restrict which types an agent may spawn use Agent(type1, type2) in its tools field - this allowlist applies ONLY to an agent running as the main thread via claude --agent; inside a subagent definition a parenthesised list is ignored. Omitting Agent from tools prevents spawning. Example (verbatim): `tools: Agent(worker, researcher), Read, Bash`. Deny specific agents: permissions.deny ["Agent(Explore)", "Agent(my-custom-agent)"] or claude --disallowedTools "Agent(Explore)"; deny rules can also match parameters: Agent(model:opus), Agent(isolation:worktree). CONCURRENCY: when 20 subagents are running, the next Agent call fails with 'Concurrent subagent limit reached' (CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS, v2.1.217+; not enforced under ultracode). Resume: each invocation creates a new instance; Claude resumes by SendMessage to the agent ID/name; transcripts at ~/.claude/projects/{project}/{sessionId}/subagents/agent-{agentId}.jsonl. FORKS (/subtask, v2.1.212+; /fork on v2.1.161-v2.1.211) inherit the whole conversation instead of starting fresh and can pass isolation: worktree.
- **Numbers:** description budget 15,000 tokens; default nesting depth 3; default concurrent subagent cap 20
- **For the fleet:** Write descriptions as mutually exclusive routing rules (one trigger each) and add 'use proactively' only on the one or two agents that should fire on their own (e.g. critic after a render). A lead run as the main session can be limited to exactly the fleet roster with Agent(render-probe, realism-critic, material-editor); leaf agents should omit Agent so they cannot fan out further. Keep nesting depth at 1-2 so cost stays predictable.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** All confirmed on the sub-agents page. Three layers below main by default; CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH set to 1 turns nesting off; 20 concurrent subagents with 'Concurrent subagent limit reached' (v2.1.217+, exempt under ultracode); the Agent(a,b) allowlist applies only to 'claude --agent' and is ignored in a subagent definition; omitting Agent prevents spawning; SendMessage resume; transcript path ~/.claude/projects/{project}/{sessionId}/subagents/agent-{agentId}.jsonl; /subtask v2.1.212+ and /fork v2.1.161-v2.1.211; @"name (agent)" syntax. Agent(model:opus) and Agent(isolation:worktree) deny rules are confirmed on the permissions page. The report omits that background-by-default has some foreground exceptions.

### Subagent tool filters, foreground/background and behaviour under claude -p

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs: sub-agents#available-tools, #run-subagents-in-foreground-or-background, tools-reference)
- **Claim:** Subagents inherit built-in + MCP tools of the main conversation, narrowed by two filters. Filter 1 removes from EVERY subagent even if listed in tools: Agent (at depth limit), AskUserQuestion, EndConversation, EnterPlanMode, ExitPlanMode (unless permissionMode is plan), ScheduleWakeup, WaitForMcpServers, Workflow. Filter 2 (background subagents) keeps every MCP tool but only these built-ins: Read, Grep, Glob, LSP, Bash, PowerShell, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit, WebFetch, WebSearch, TodoWrite, Skill, ToolSearch, EnterWorktree, ExitWorktree, Monitor, TaskStop, SendMessage, Artifact (+SubagentHandback); the same definition can therefore resolve to different tools in foreground vs background. FOREGROUND vs BACKGROUND: where fork mode is on (default in interactive sessions, v2.1.232+) every subagent Claude spawns runs in the background and Claude cannot ask for the foreground; where fork mode is off (default in claude -p and the Agent SDK unless CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT=1) Claude runs subagents in the background by default and in the foreground when it needs the result; frontmatter background: true pins one to the background; CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1 forces foreground. Permission prompts: foreground = shown at the moment; background = surfaced in the main session naming the subagent (Esc denies that one call). In a -p run there is no human, so prompts are denied (see headless finding). Subagent permissionMode: if the main session is in bypassPermissions/acceptEdits/auto, the subagent runs in that same mode and its own setting is ignored; if main is default/dontAsk/plan, the subagent's setting applies (bypassPermissions is never granted to a subagent unless main has it, v2.1.267+). Built-in tool-less guard: if every entry in tools fails to match, the Agent call errors ('Agent would be spawned with zero tools').
- **Numbers:** fork mode: default on (interactive, v2.1.232+), off in -p; 8 filtered tool names for all subagents
- **For the fleet:** Subagents cannot ask the owner questions (no AskUserQuestion) and cannot launch workflows; only the main thread can. Anything that must be a hard gate (no push, no render on the wrong machine) must therefore be a permission rule or hook, not a prompt instruction. Design each agent so it works with the background tool set (Read/Grep/Glob/Bash/Edit/Write/WebFetch/Skill) because that is what it gets by default.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed. The first filter removes AskUserQuestion, EndConversation, EnterPlanMode, ExitPlanMode (unless permissionMode is plan), ScheduleWakeup, WaitForMcpServers, Workflow, and Agent at the depth limit. The background keep-list matches, including Artifact and SubagentHandback. Fork mode is on by default in interactive sessions (v2.1.232+) and off in -p unless CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT=1. CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1 forces foreground. Subagent permission-mode inheritance and the v2.1.267 bypassPermissions rule match. Background prompts surface in the main session and Esc denies one call. The zero-tools error exists (the exact error string is not quoted on the page).

### Subagent image reading (Read tool on PNG frames)

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/tools-reference (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs: tools-reference#read-tool-behavior; sub-agents#available-tools)
- **Claim:** CONFIRMED in docs: the Read tool returns image files (PNG, JPG and other formats) 'as visual content that Claude can see, not as raw bytes'. Claude Code resizes/recompresses large images to fit model limits, so Claude may see a downscaled version; an image still larger than 500KB after resize is re-encoded as JPEG at reduced quality with unchanged pixel dimensions. If fine pixel detail is missed, the docs advise asking Claude to crop the region first, e.g. with ImageMagick via Bash. Images, PDFs and notebooks keep their own limits (not lifted by allow_large). PDFs use pdftoppm (poppler-utils) for page ranges. CONFIRMED: subagents inherit Read (it is not in any subagent filter list, and it is in the background-subagent keep list). NOT STATED EXPLICITLY in any page read: the sentence 'a subagent can view PNGs'. It follows from the two facts above (a subagent's Read call returns the same visual content into the subagent's own context; only the subagent's final text returns to the parent, so the parent never sees the pixels). Whether a haiku-class model reads fine render artefacts well enough is a quality question the docs do not answer. Minimal agent: tools: Read (plus Grep/Glob), model: sonnet, prompt 'Read each PNG path given; report defects'.
- **Numbers:** re-encode to JPEG when image >500KB after resize; PDF reads up to 20 pages per call
- **For the fleet:** Put the image-judging step in a Read-equipped subagent and have it return text (defects + frame filename), so frames never bloat the lead's context. Give it absolute PNG paths, a few frames at a time, and (if detail matters) a crop step first. Treat 'subagents can see images' as inferred, and verify once on the owner's PC with a single test frame before relying on it.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** false
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: partly
- **Correction:** The documented parts are confirmed in tools-reference under 'Read tool behavior'. PNG/JPG are returned as visual content; large images are resized and recompressed; an image still over 500KB is re-encoded as JPEG with the same pixel dimensions; ImageMagick cropping is suggested; PDFs use pdftoppm with up to 20 pages per call; allow_large (v2.1.296+) does not lift image or PDF limits. The sentence 'a subagent can view PNGs' is stated nowhere, and the report correctly marks it inferred. Haiku-class image quality is also unanswered by the docs. The report's confirmed_from_primary_source=false is accurate.

### Git worktrees: --worktree, subagent isolation: worktree, base branch and cleanup

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs, worktrees page + settings-reference (worktree.*))
- **Claim:** CLI: `claude --worktree feature-auth` (or -w; name optional, e.g. generated 'bright-running-fox') creates <repo>/.claude/worktrees/<name>/ on a new branch worktree-<name> and starts Claude there; `claude -w feature-auth --tmux` adds a tmux session; `claude --worktree "#1234"` branches from PR 1234. Interactive runs need workspace trust accepted once; claude -p skips the trust check. Add .claude/worktrees/ to .gitignore. SUBAGENTS: add `isolation: worktree` to the agent frontmatter (verbatim docs example: name: refactorer, description: Applies mechanical refactors across many files, isolation: worktree) or tell Claude 'use worktrees for your agents'. Each subagent gets a temporary worktree that is removed automatically if it finishes with no changes; a worktree with changes stays until a periodic sweep removes it (older than cleanupPeriodDays, never if it still holds changed/untracked files or unpushed commits, never your own git worktree add trees). Claude Code holds a git worktree lock on a running agent's tree. BASE BRANCH (important): worktree.baseRef defaults to "fresh" = branch from origin/<default branch> (clean tree matching the remote); set {"worktree":{"baseRef":"head"}} to branch from your current local HEAD so unpushed commits / the feature branch are present. Subagent worktrees use the same rule. CLEANUP: interactive exit removes a clean unnamed worktree automatically, prompts for a named one or one with work; claude -p has NO exit prompt, so its worktrees are not cleaned (remove with `git worktree remove <path>`, `git worktree unlock` first if locked). OTHER: .worktreeinclude (gitignore syntax) copies gitignored files such as .env into every new worktree (also subagent worktrees); worktree.symlinkDirectories (array of repo-relative dirs, e.g. ["node_modules",".cache"]) symlinks big dirs instead of copying; worktree.sparsePaths = sparse checkout; repo-local git filter drivers (e.g. git lfs install --local) are NOT run, so LFS files appear as pointer files until `git lfs pull`. A subagent in its own worktree takes CLAUDE.md/rules from the MAIN conversation, not from its worktree. Isolation checks block Edit/Write/NotebookEdit to the main checkout and git redirects into it, but not cp/redirects. Hook ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR} stays at the main checkout; hook JSON field cwd follows the worktree. "Yes, don't ask again" Bash approvals made in a worktree are saved to the MAIN checkout's .claude/settings.local.json. Untracked .claude/skills|agents|commands in the main checkout are read through when the worktree has none.
- **Numbers:** none (cleanup age = cleanupPeriodDays; value not read)
- **For the fleet:** Parallel edit agents get conflict-free copies for free with `isolation: worktree`, but ONLY if worktree.baseRef is "head" in .claude/settings.json (otherwise they branch from origin/main and miss the lead's unpushed work). Heavy untracked trees the render needs (a .venv or Isaac Sim symlink at repo root, if present - unconfirmed for this repo) should be listed in worktree.symlinkDirectories. Edit agents should commit inside their worktree and report the branch/commit; the lead then merges only verified improvements. Unattended -p loops must clean their own worktrees.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed on the worktrees page. .claude/worktrees/<name>, branch worktree-<name>, the '#1234' PR form, the trust rule with -p skipping it, the 'refactorer' example, baseRef fresh/head, no cleanup under -p (git worktree unlock first), .worktreeinclude, the LFS pointer-file note, CLAUDE.md taken from the main conversation, isolation checks not covering cp or redirects, CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR staying put with hook cwd following the worktree, and 'don't ask again' approvals going to the main checkout's settings.local.json. Two items sit on other pages. '--tmux' is on cli-reference and requires --worktree. worktree.symlinkDirectories and worktree.sparsePaths are in settings-reference (arrays of repo-relative directories, default unset). cleanupPeriodDays defaults to 30, which the report said it had not read.

### Skills: .claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md frontmatter, invocation, arguments, supporting files

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs, skills page (fetched except last ~9.8k chars))
- **Claim:** LOCATIONS: enterprise (managed) > personal ~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md > project .claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md (committed); nested <subdir>/.claude/skills/ load when Claude first reads/edits a file there; plugin <plugin>/skills/. Old .claude/commands/<name>.md still works (same frontmatter except name and paths); a skill wins over a command with the same name. Live reload: skill dirs are watched; for a brand-new top-level skills dir run /reload-skills. FRONTMATTER (all optional, description recommended): name (defaults to directory name), description (combined description+when_to_use truncated at 1,536 chars in the listing), when_to_use, argument-hint (e.g. [issue-number]), arguments (named positional args; space string or YAML list), disable-model-invocation (true = only the user can invoke with /name; also blocks preloading into subagents), user-invocable (false = hidden from / menu, Claude-only), allowed-tools (pre-approves tools for the turn that invokes the skill; clears at next user message; does NOT restrict other tools; space/comma string or YAML list), disallowed-tools (removes tools while the skill is active), model (override for the rest of the current turn; same values as /model, or inherit), effort (low|medium|high|xhigh|max), context (fork = run in a subagent), agent (subagent type for context: fork; default general-purpose), background (with fork; default true; false waits; v2.1.218+), hooks, paths (glob patterns that limit auto-activation), shell (bash default | powershell), metadata, license, compatibility. Booleans accept true/false/yes/no/on/off/1/0. INVOKE: type /<name> args at the start of a message, or let Claude load it from the description; works in claude -p by putting /skill-name in the prompt string. SUBSTITUTIONS: $ARGUMENTS (all; if no placeholder, 'ARGUMENTS: <value>' is appended), $ARGUMENTS[N] and $N (0-based), $name (declared in arguments), ${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}, ${CLAUDE_EFFORT}, ${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}, ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}; escape a literal $ as \$. DYNAMIC CONTEXT: !`command` (or a ```! block) runs before Claude sees the skill and its output replaces the placeholder; a failing command aborts the invocation (disableSkillShellExecution can turn this off). SUPPORTING FILES: keep SKILL.md <500 lines; put reference.md, examples.md, scripts/helper.py beside it and reference them. Permission rules: Skill(name) exact, Skill(name *) prefix, bare Skill = deny all skills.

MINIMAL EXAMPLE (.claude/skills/probe-render/SKILL.md; composed from documented fields, not executed; the command is the one in the repo's CLAUDE.md):
---
name: probe-render
description: Run the quick probe render of the drone scene on this PC and report the log lines that matter.
argument-hint: [route] [seconds]
arguments: [route, seconds]
disable-model-invocation: true
allowed-tools:
  - Bash(./isaaclab.sh *)
  - Read
---
Run ./isaaclab.sh -p scripts/drone_scene/quadcopter_pbr.py --route $route --headless --record $seconds --cameras front back --resolution_scale 0.5 , then read drone_scene_videos/drone_scene.log and report HdrColor status, the mean scene radiance line, camera mount lines, any [rtx_look] WARNING lines and any traceback.
Invoke: /probe-render straight 60
- **Numbers:** SKILL.md guideline <500 lines; description+when_to_use truncated at 1,536 chars
- **For the fleet:** Use skills for the repeatable procedures (probe render + log check, critique rubric, commit-if-verified) and mark side-effecting ones disable-model-invocation: true so only the owner or the lead's explicit instruction triggers a GPU render. Skills live in the same repo folder and are shared on git pull. Remember allowed-tools only pre-approves; it is not a sandbox, and it lapses at the next user message.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed. Locations and precedence, the frontmatter list, the 1,536-character truncation, the 500-line guideline, all substitutions, !`cmd` injection (a failing command aborts the invocation), allowed-tools (space/comma string or YAML list; only the invoking turn; not a restriction), the Skill(name) and Skill(name *) rules, and 'ARGUMENTS: <value>' being appended when there is no placeholder. The skills page also says disable-model-invocation: true stops Claude from invoking the skill, stops preloading it into subagents, and stops a scheduled task firing it. That matters for a design rule below. The last ~9.8k characters of the page were not read.

### Skills with context: fork and agent (skill running as a subagent)

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs: skills#run-skills-in-a-subagent)
- **Claim:** `context: fork` starts a new subagent of the type named in `agent` (built-ins Explore, Plan, general-purpose, OR any custom subagent from .claude/agents/; default general-purpose) and gives it the SKILL.md content as its prompt. The forked subagent does not see the conversation history (so the skill text must be self-contained) and is NOT the same as a /subtask fork. It runs in the background (result arrives when done) unless `background: false`; in claude -p / Agent SDK, with CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1, or when the same skill is already running, Claude Code waits for the result. System prompt comes from the agent type; CLAUDE.md loads per that agent's startup context (Explore and Plan skip CLAUDE.md and git status, so a forked skill with agent: Explore sees none of your CLAUDE.md). `model:` in the skill sets the forked subagent's model. Verbatim docs example: ---\nname: deep-research\ndescription: Research a topic thoroughly\ncontext: fork\nagent: Explore\n---\nResearch $ARGUMENTS thoroughly: 1. Find relevant files using Glob and Grep 2. Read and analyze the code 3. Summarize findings with specific file references. Messages from forked skills in stream-json carry parent_tool_use_id (a 'forked-command-' prefix when the user typed /skill-name as the prompt, v2.1.287+). Guideline-only skills (no actionable task) return little under context: fork. The opposite direction also exists: an agent's `skills:` frontmatter preloads full skill bodies into that agent.
- **Numbers:** none
- **For the fleet:** A skill is the cleanest way to give the owner a one-word command (/critique-frames path) that runs a specific project subagent with a fixed rubric: `context: fork` + `agent: realism-critic`. The rubric lives in one place and the cheap model choice comes from the agent file.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed. Verbatim 'deep-research' Explore example; the forked subagent gets the skill content as its prompt and no conversation history; background by default with background: false to wait; waits in -p, in the Agent SDK, and under CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1; Explore and Plan start without CLAUDE.md and git status. The forked-command- prefix (v2.1.287+) is confirmed on the headless page.

### Dynamic workflows: saved scripts in .claude/workflows/ (publicly documented)

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/workflows (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs workflows page; agent-sdk/typescript#workflow; settings-reference; tools-reference)
- **Claim:** PUBLIC AND DOCUMENTED (not hidden). A dynamic workflow is a JavaScript script that orchestrates many subagents in the background; Claude writes it for your task and a runtime executes it while the session stays responsive. Available on all paid plans/API/Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry (Pro: turn on in /config). Plan lives in code: script variables hold intermediate results, so only the final answer lands in Claude's context. FILE FORMAT: a .js file in .claude/workflows/ (project, shared with everyone who clones the repo; closest .claude/workflows/ to cwd wins, project beats personal) or ~/.claude/workflows/ (personal), or a plugin workflows/ dir (namespaced /plugin:name). Starts with a plain-literal `export const meta = { name, description }` (optional phases list; anything non-literal drops it from / autocomplete), then a body of plain JavaScript with top-level await: agent(prompt, opts) spawns one subagent (documented opts: schema = JSON-schema for structured output, retried up to 5x, MAX_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_RETRIES; label; stallMs), pipeline(items, fn) one agent per item, parallel(...) runs agent tasks concurrently, phase(title), log(msg), global `args`. No import(), no filesystem/shell from the script itself (agents do the work), Date.now()/Math.random()/new Date() throw (pass timestamps via args), no mid-run user input. Verbatim docs example:
export const meta = {
  name: 'audit-routes',
  description: 'Audit every route handler for missing auth checks',
}
const found = await agent('List every .ts file under src/routes/.', {
  schema: { type: 'object', required: ['files'], properties: { files: { type: 'array', items: { type: 'string' } } } },
})
const audits = await pipeline(found.files, file =>
  agent(`Audit ${file} for missing authentication checks.`, { label: file }),
)
return audits.filter(Boolean)
INVOCATION: saved workflows appear as /<name> commands in autocomplete; /workflows lists runs (keys: arrows, Enter, f filter, p pause/resume, x stop, r restart agent, s save run as command); save a run: /workflows > select > s > Tab toggles project vs personal; the Workflow tool input is {script?, name?, scriptPath?, args?, resumeFromRunId?} (Agent SDK v0.3.149+; name = built-in or saved workflow). Pass input via args (e.g. 'Run /triage-issues on issues 1024, 1025, and 1030'). Edit a saved script by hand or ask Claude; load the script-writing reference with the bundled /workflow-authoring skill (v2.1.248+); /reload-skills re-reads workflow dirs. OPT-IN RULES: (a) ask in your own words ('use a workflow') or include the keyword `ultracode` in a prompt you type (interactive prompt, IDE panel, Remote Control, or SDK input stamped origin {kind:'human'}); the keyword does NOT trigger from claude -p prompts, unstamped SDK prompts, scheduled tasks, webhooks/PR comments (since v2.1.210); (b) /effort ultracode or claude --effort ultracode (also sets xhigh) or settings key "ultracode": true makes Claude plan a workflow for every substantive task (the owner's settings already contain an ultracode key; value not visible); (c) running a saved/bundled workflow by name. APPROVAL: CLI shows a per-run prompt (Yes run it / Yes and don't ask again for <name> in <path> / View raw script / No) in manual & acceptEdits mode; auto mode asks only on first launch (skipped under ultracode); bypassPermissions never asks; in claude -p and the Agent SDK there is no prompt, so the launch goes through normal permission evaluation: add `Workflow` or `Workflow(<name>)` to permissions.allow, or use auto/bypass mode, or a PreToolUse hook returning allow. Workflow agents use your permission rules; their model follows the subagent model order (a model the script names counts as per-invocation). LIMITS: 16 concurrent agents by default (CLAUDE_CODE_WORKFLOW_MAX_CONCURRENT_AGENTS 1-256, v2.1.269+), 1,000 agents per run, 4,096 items per parallel()/pipeline(), 'Large workflow' warning at >25 agents or >1.5M projected tokens; runs resumable within the same session (completed agents return cached results until the first changed prompt); pauses (not fails) on claude.ai usage limits only in interactive subscription sessions; stalled agent auto-restarts up to 5 times. Size guideline setting workflowSizeGuideline: unrestricted | small (<5 agents) | medium (<10, default) | large (<50). Off switches: enableWorkflows/disableWorkflows setting, CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_WORKFLOWS=1. Subagents can never call Workflow. NOT CONFIRMED IN PUBLIC DOCS: the option names agent() accepts for per-agent model, subagent/agent type (docs only say agents with the same 'model, effort level, agent type, tools, output schema, working directory' share prompt cache) and worktree isolation (an example prompt says 'working on each file in its own isolated copy'); the parallel() signature; whether `claude -p "/<saved-workflow>"` runs a saved workflow directly. The /workflow-authoring bundled skill holds that reference (it is installed in the owner's environment).
- **Numbers:** 16 concurrent agents default (1-256 via env); 1,000 agents/run; 4,096 items per parallel/pipeline; Large-workflow warning >25 agents or >1.5M tokens; structured output retries 5; stall restarts up to 5; size guideline small<5, medium<10, large<50 agents
- **For the fleet:** A saved workflow is the right container for the fixed loop (render -> critique -> edit in parallel -> re-render serially -> keep verified): the script, not a context window, holds the loop and the GPU-serialization order, and it is committed to git and runs as /<name>. Do not hand-write option names for model/agent-type/isolation from memory: have Claude (with /workflow-authoring loaded) generate the first version, test it on one small slice, then save it with `s` and commit .claude/workflows/<name>.js. For unattended runs add an allow rule Workflow(<name>) and set ultracode off for that run.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed on the workflows page. It covers: meta literal; the verbatim audit-routes example; agent() opts schema/label/stallMs; pipeline, parallel, phase, log, args; Date.now, Math.random and no-arg new Date() throw; no import(); 16 concurrent agents (1-256, v2.1.269+); 1,000 agents per run; 4,096 items; the Large workflow warning at >25 agents or >1.5M tokens; retries; stall restarts up to 5; approval-by-mode table; allow rules Workflow and Workflow(<name>); the ultracode keyword not working from -p, scheduled tasks or webhooks (since v2.1.210); /workflows keys; the save dialog; /workflow-authoring (v2.1.248+). The Workflow tool input in the TypeScript SDK reference matches (v0.3.149+; title and description are ignored). Corrections: (1) the workflowSizeGuideline default is medium, or small when signed in on a Pro plan with v2.1.271+. (2) The statement that the owner's ~/.claude/settings.json already contains an 'ultracode' key is an environment claim I cannot verify from documentation, and so is the claim that /workflow-authoring is installed in the owner's environment. The public docs do show that a script can name a model, since 'a model the script names for a stage counts as the per-invocation model', but not the option name. The report's open questions on option names and on 'claude -p /<workflow>' stand.

### Agent teams (experimental): enable, display modes, limits, vs subagents

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-teams (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs agent-teams page)
- **Claim:** WHAT: several full Claude Code sessions coordinated by one lead; teammates have their own context windows, message each other directly, and share a task list (pending / in progress / completed, with dependencies and file-lock claiming) when the session has the Task tools. EXPERIMENTAL AND OFF BY DEFAULT. ENABLE: set CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1 in the shell or in settings.json: {"env":{"CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS":"1"}}; set it to "0" to turn off. Side effect: while enabled, a subagent that Claude gives a name to launches as a TEAMMATE (teams can form without being asked). Spawn by prompt, e.g. 'Spawn three teammates to explore this from different angles...' or 'Spawn a teammate using the security-reviewer agent type to audit the auth module'. Teammate model order: model named in the spawn prompt > the subagent definition's model (inherit = lead) > CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL > lead's model. A named subagent definition contributes tools, model, disallowedTools, effort, body (appended for in-process, replaces default prompt for split-pane); its skills are NOT applied (teammate loads project/user skills); mcpServers only for split-pane. Display: teammateMode in settings (in-process default | auto | tmux | iterm2) or claude --teammate-mode; split panes need tmux or iTerm2+it2 and are not supported in VS Code's integrated terminal, Windows Terminal or Ghostty. Controls: arrows/Enter in agent panel, x stops a teammate, Ctrl+T task list. Hooks: TeammateIdle, TaskCreated, TaskCompleted (exit 2 blocks/keeps working). STORAGE: ~/.claude/teams/{team}/config.json (removed at session end; do not edit), ~/.claude/tasks/{team}/ (kept). WHEN TO USE vs SUBAGENTS: teams for research/review with debate, independent modules, competing-hypothesis debugging, cross-layer work where teammates must talk; subagents for focused workers that just report back (lower token cost). Recommended team size 3-5 teammates, 5-6 tasks each. LIMITATIONS: no /resume or /rewind of in-process teammates; task status can lag; slow shutdown; one team per session; no nested teams (teammates cannot spawn teammates); in-process teammates cannot run background subagents; lead fixed; permission mode is the lead's at spawn (dontAsk not inherited; no per-teammate mode at spawn); teammates are NOT isolated in worktrees (partition files); needs an interactive session - under claude -p a named subagent runs as an ordinary subagent; significantly higher token use; teammate permission prompts bubble to the lead.
- **Numbers:** recommended 3-5 teammates, 5-6 tasks per teammate; idle rows hide after 30 s; idle rows beyond 3 collapse
- **For the fleet:** Agent teams are a poor fit for this fleet: the work is a fixed pipeline around one serialized GPU, teams need an interactive session (no overnight -p), do not give teammates worktrees, and cost more. Prefer subagents (cheap, isolated, resumable) plus a workflow script for the loop. Keep CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS unset or "0" so named subagents do not silently become teammates.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: partly
- **Correction:** Almost everything is confirmed. It is experimental and off by default; CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1 via settings env; a named subagent launches as a teammate while it is on; the teammate model order; the subagent-definition fields that apply (skills are NOT applied; mcpServers only for split-pane); teammateMode values; ~/.claude/teams and ~/.claude/tasks; 3-5 teammates and 5-6 tasks each; 30 s idle-row hiding; the limitations list; hooks; split panes unsupported in VS Code's terminal, Windows Terminal and Ghostty; under -p a named subagent runs as an ordinary subagent. One overstatement: 'teammates are NOT isolated in worktrees' is not stated. The page only says to partition files between teammates to avoid conflicts, and that a spawn call passing isolation is not a teammate. The conclusion (prefer subagents plus a workflow) is a judgment, not a docs fact.

### Running the main session as a custom agent (--agent / agent setting)

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs: sub-agents#invoke-subagents-explicitly, cli-reference, settings-reference#agent)
- **Claim:** `claude --agent code-reviewer` starts a session whose main thread takes on that agent's tool restrictions and model. Unless the agent's prompt body is empty, the agent's system prompt REPLACES the default Claude Code system prompt entirely (like --system-prompt). CLAUDE.md files and project memory still load (even if omitClaudeMd is set, which is ignored for the main session agent). The name shows as @<name> in the startup header; the choice persists across resume (tool restrictions and model restored; warning if the agent file vanished). Plugin agents: pass the name, or plugin:name to disambiguate. DEFAULT FOR A PROJECT: in .claude/settings.json {"agent": "code-reviewer"}; the setting also sets the default agent for sessions dispatched from `claude agents`; the --agent flag overrides the setting. Frontmatter effects when used as main: initialPrompt is auto-submitted as the first user turn (commands and skills are processed, prepended to any prompt you type); autoCompactWindow has no effect; frontmatter hooks fire alongside settings.json hooks; with -p and an agent that has its own prompt, the git-status snapshot is skipped. The Agent(type, ...) allowlist in tools applies only in this main-thread mode. Verbatim docs example:
---
name: coordinator
description: Coordinates work across specialized agents
tools: Agent(worker, researcher), Read, Bash
---
MINIMAL FLEET LEAD (composed, not executed): .claude/agents/fleet-lead.md with `tools: Agent(render-probe, realism-critic, material-editor), Read, Grep, Glob, Bash`, `model: opus`, `effort: high`, body = the loop procedure; run `claude --agent fleet-lead`. Permission mode for the main-session case is not described on the subagent page (the session's own mode applies via --permission-mode / settings).
- **Numbers:** none
- **For the fleet:** Make the lead an agent file and start it with `claude --agent fleet-lead` (CLI) or set "agent": "fleet-lead" in .claude/settings.json; the Agent(...) allowlist then pins the roster. Because its body replaces the default system prompt, copy the essential working rules (run only on this PC, ask before push, plain exact explanations) into the body even though CLAUDE.md also loads.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed on the sub-agents page and settings-reference. The system prompt replaces the default unless the body is empty; CLAUDE.md still loads; omitClaudeMd is ignored for the main-session agent; initialPrompt is auto-submitted; autoCompactWindow has no effect; frontmatter hooks run alongside settings hooks; @<name> in the header; the choice persists on resume with a warning if the agent file is gone; plugin:name disambiguation; the 'agent' key in .claude/settings.json; the git-status snapshot is skipped under -p with an agent that has its own prompt; the verbatim coordinator example. Unverified detail: the claim that the 'agent' setting is also the default for sessions dispatched from 'claude agents'. The sub-agents page does not mention it; cli-reference says only that 'claude agents' accepts --agent to set defaults.

### Output styles (.claude/output-styles/*.md) for how the main assistant talks

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/output-styles (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs output-styles page)
- **Claim:** An output style is instructions that set Claude's role, tone and format for every response in a session. Built-ins: Default, Proactive, Concise (v2.1.237+), Explanatory, Learning. CUSTOM: a Markdown file at ~/.claude/output-styles/, project .claude/output-styles/ (loads from every such dir between cwd and repo root; closest wins), or managed dir; plugins can ship output-styles/. FRONTMATTER (all optional, hyphenated): name (default = file name), description, keep-coding-instructions (true keeps Claude Code's software-engineering instruction section; default false), force-for-plugin (plugin styles only). Select: /output-style <name> (case-insensitive; also works in -p, v2.1.269+; saves to .claude/settings.local.json), /config > Output style, VS Code command menu '/' > Output styles (v2.1.257+), or settings key "outputStyle": "<name>" (value is CASE-SENSITIVE; a non-matching value silently gives Default). A project .claude/settings.json outputStyle overrides the user default. Terminal reads style files at startup - restart after creating/editing one. How it works: the active style's instructions are sent with every request; custom styles drop the built-in coding instructions unless keep-coding-instructions: true (that field only matters on the full system prompt; CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE_SYSTEM_PROMPT=0 selects it). Styles apply to the main conversation and to forks, NOT to other subagents (they run their own system prompt). A style is an instruction, not enforcement; use CLAUDE.md for project facts, hooks for must-happen rules, skills for task procedures. Verbatim docs example:
---
name: Diagrams first
description: Lead every explanation with a diagram
keep-coding-instructions: true
---
When explaining code, architecture, or data flow, start with a Mermaid diagram showing the structure, then explain in prose.

MINIMAL OWNER-RULES STYLE (composed, not executed): .claude/output-styles/plain-exact.md with `name: plain-exact`, `keep-coding-instructions: true` and body 'Explain plainly and exactly. Give full absolute paths, say which machine and folder you mean, and give only commands that were actually checked; label anything unchecked as unchecked.'; enable with "outputStyle": "plain-exact" in .claude/settings.json.
- **Numbers:** none
- **For the fleet:** Encode the owner's explanation rules (plain, exact, full paths, which machine, only verified commands) as a committed output style for the lead session, with keep-coding-instructions: true. Because styles do not reach subagents, repeat one sentence of the same rule in each agent's body so their reports follow it too.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed. The five styles, with Concise at v2.1.237+. Locations at user, project and managed level, with plugin support. Frontmatter name, description, keep-coding-instructions and force-for-plugin. /output-style saves to .claude/settings.local.json and works in -p from v2.1.269. The VS Code menu needs v2.1.257 (v2.1.261 to create a style). The outputStyle value is case-sensitive and a mismatch gives Default. Restart the terminal after editing a style file. Styles apply to the main conversation and to forks, not to other subagents. The 'Diagrams first' example is verbatim. CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE_SYSTEM_PROMPT=0 selects the full prompt.

### Hooks: PreToolUse contract (stdin JSON, exit 2, permissionDecision ask/deny/allow), matchers and if

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs hooks reference + hooks-guide (reference fetched through char ~200k of ~274k))
- **Claim:** CONFIG (settings.json at ~/.claude/, .claude/ (shared), .claude/settings.local.json, managed, plugin hooks/hooks.json, or skill/subagent frontmatter): three levels event > matcher group > handler. Verbatim docs shape:
{"hooks":{"PreToolUse":[{"matcher":"Bash","hooks":[{"type":"command","command":"\"$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR\"/.claude/hooks/protect-files.sh"}]}]}}
Handler fields: type (command | http | mcp_tool | prompt | agent), if (ONE permission-rule string, tool events only, best-effort e.g. "Bash(git *)" or "Edit(*.ts)"), timeout (s; default 600 command/http/mcp_tool, 30 prompt, 60 agent), statusMessage, once (skill frontmatter only); command hooks also args, async, asyncRewake, shell, onFailure. Matcher: "*"/""/omitted = all; only letters, digits, _, -, space, comma, | = exact names list; anything else = unanchored JS regex (^Edit$ for whole-string). Scripts must be executable (chmod +x). SCRIPT CONTRACT: stdin = JSON {session_id, prompt_id, transcript_path, cwd, scratchpad_dir, permission_mode, hook_event_name, tool_name, tool_input (Bash: command, description, timeout, run_in_background), tool_use_id}; Write/Edit/Read tool_input.file_path is absolute; cwd follows the worktree/cd. EXIT CODES: 0 = success, stdout JSON is parsed (plain-text stdout only becomes context on UserPromptSubmit/UserPromptExpansion/SessionStart/PostModelSwitch); 2 = blocking error (tool call stopped, stderr is fed to Claude as the reason, JSON on stdout cannot override, even "allow"); any other code = NON-blocking error (action proceeds) - so use exit 2 to enforce. Choose either exit 2 or exit 0 + JSON per hook. PreToolUse JSON (verbatim): {"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"Destructive command blocked by hook"}} ; permissionDecision values: allow (skip the prompt), deny (cancel; reason goes to Claude), ask (show the normal permission prompt to the user; reason shown in prompt), defer (-p only: exit with tool call preserved for an SDK wrapper). Optional updatedInput (REPLACES the whole tool input; permission rules are evaluated against the returned input) and additionalContext. Top-level decision/reason on PreToolUse is deprecated (approve->allow, block->deny). Several hooks run in parallel; conflicting PreToolUse decisions resolve deny > defer > ask > allow. A hook never bypasses permission rules: a matching deny rule still blocks and a matching ask rule still prompts even if the hook says allow/ask; an exit-2 hook blocks even when an allow rule matches. OTHER EVENTS: Stop / SubagentStop use {"decision":"block","reason":"..."} to keep going (input has stop_hook_active; 8-consecutive-continuation cap, CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP); SubagentStart (agent_id, agent_type; context only) / SubagentStop (also agent_transcript_path, last_assistant_message); TaskCompleted/TaskCreated/TeammateIdle exit 2 blocks; WorktreeCreate (command prints the path; any non-zero exit fails) / WorktreeRemove; SessionStart (startup|resume|clear|compact|fork). Subagent-frontmatter hooks (docs example): hooks: PreToolUse: - matcher: "Bash" hooks: - type: command command: "./scripts/validate-readonly-query.sh". Env available to hooks: CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR (stays at main checkout in a worktree), CLAUDE_EFFORT, CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE ("true" in remote web environments, unset in the local CLI), CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT/DATA. /hooks browses configured hooks; claude --debug shows hook output; disableAllHooks turns them off. Under claude -p (without --bare) project .claude/settings.json hooks run even in a folder never trusted.
- **Numbers:** timeouts: 600 s command, 30 s prompt, 60 s agent; Stop-hook continuation cap 8; precedence deny > defer > ask > allow
- **For the fleet:** Hooks are the enforcement layer for the owner's rules (they run in Claude Code, not in the model): a PreToolUse Bash hook can force confirmation for git push (ask), hard-block render commands when CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE=true, and (via updatedInput) wrap `./isaaclab.sh` in `flock /tmp/gpu.lock` so renders are serialized even if two agents try at once - that last use is our design, built on the documented updatedInput mechanism, and is untested. Always use exit 2 (not exit 1) for hard blocks and keep hook scripts committed and executable.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
