- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed. Reference read through ~char 200k of 273,658; the last ~73k was not read. The three-level shape; the handler types; the timeouts 600/30/60; one permission rule in 'if'; the matcher rules; the exit codes 0/2/other; exit 2 with valid JSON cannot be overridden even by allow; plain-text stdout is context only on UserPromptSubmit, UserPromptExpansion, SessionStart and PostModelSwitch; permissionDecision allow/deny/ask/defer (defer only in -p); updatedInput replaces the whole input; the deprecated top-level decision (approve->allow, block->deny); deny > defer > ask > allow; Stop and SubagentStop block with stop_hook_active and the 8-continuation cap (CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP); CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE is 'true' in remote web environments and unset in the local CLI; WorktreeCreate and WorktreeRemove behaviour; frontmatter hooks (Stop becomes SubagentStop). The protect-files.sh example is in the hooks-guide page, not the reference. Not verified: the handler fields async/asyncRewake/shell/onFailure and the SessionStart matcher list. Also documented, and relevant below: permission rules are evaluated against the input the hook returns.

### Enforcing 'ask before git push': permissions.ask rule plus PreToolUse hook

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs: permissions, permission-modes, settings-reference#permissions-ask, hooks-guide)
- **Claim:** DOCUMENTED RULE: settings key permissions.ask lists tool uses that prompt even in a mode that would otherwise approve them (acceptEdits, bypassPermissions); in dontAsk mode a matching call is denied instead. Verbatim docs example: {"permissions":{"ask":["Bash(git push *)"]}}. Evaluation order is deny, then ask, then allow; first match wins regardless of specificity; an allow rule cannot carve an exception out of a deny. CAVEAT ABOUT DEFAULT MODE: since v2.1.283 AUTO mode is the built-in starting mode for interactive terminal and VS Code sessions, and auto mode lets pushes to branches of the repository you are working in run WITHOUT a prompt (unless on the classifier's blocked list); the docs say to add permissions.ask rules for a human checkpoint, and that ask rules matching a command's content (Bash(git push *)) fall back to a permission prompt even in auto mode. A boundary stated only in conversation ('don't push') is read by the classifier but is not stored and can be lost on compaction; for a hard guarantee use a deny rule. BASH RULE LIMITS (documented): rules match the command AS WRITTEN: Bash(git push *) matches `git push origin main` but NOT `git -C . push origin main`, `git -c push.default=current push origin main`, or `git 'push' origin main`; the space before the trailing * matters (Bash(git push*) would also match git push-foo); `:*` suffix is an equivalent legacy form of ` *`; compound commands are split on && || ; | |& & and newlines and each part is matched; deny/ask apply if ANY subcommand matches, including inside $() and subshells; wrappers timeout/time/nice/nohup/stdbuf/command/builtin and bare xargs are stripped. In claude -p a request that would prompt has no human to ask and is denied.

MINIMAL WORKING CONFIG (.claude/settings.json; composed from documented fields and the verbatim hook output shape, NOT executed):
{
  "permissions": {
    "ask": ["Bash(git push *)"],
    "deny": ["Bash(git push --force *)", "Bash(git push -f *)"]
  },
  "hooks": {
    "PreToolUse": [
      { "matcher": "Bash",
        "hooks": [ { "type": "command", "if": "Bash(git *)",
                     "command": "\"$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR\"/.claude/hooks/ask-on-push.sh" } ] }
    ]
  }
}
.claude/hooks/ask-on-push.sh (chmod +x; needs jq, as in the docs' own scripts):
#!/bin/bash
INPUT=$(cat)
CMD=$(echo "$INPUT" | jq -r '.tool_input.command // empty')
if echo "$CMD" | grep -Eq '\bgit\b.*\bpush\b'; then
  echo '{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"ask","permissionDecisionReason":"git push needs the owner approval"}}'
fi
exit 0
Hard-block variant (documented pattern): print a reason to stderr and `exit 2`. Machine guard (composed): `if [ "$CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE" = "true" ] && echo "$CMD" | grep -Eq 'isaaclab\.sh|quadcopter_pbr'; then echo 'Blocked: renders run only on the owner PC' >&2; exit 2; fi`.
- **Numbers:** none
- **For the fleet:** CLAUDE.md's 'Ask before pushing' is only an instruction, and with auto mode as the default start mode a same-repo push can go through silently. Commit BOTH the permissions.ask rule (catches the plain form) and the regex hook (catches git -C/-c variants) in .claude/settings.json. For unattended loops, also put Bash(git push *) in deny so it can never run. The CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE guard is a belt-and-braces check for the 'never render in the cloud' rule (documented env var; behaviour on the owner PC is unset, so the guard is inert there).
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed on permissions, permission-modes and settings-reference. Evaluation order is deny, ask, allow with the first match winning. Bash(git push *) does not match 'git -C . push', 'git -c push.default=current push' or "git 'push'" (the docs table says so). The wrapper list is timeout, time, nice, nohup, stdbuf, command, builtin, noglob and bare xargs. Compound commands are split, and deny/ask apply if any subcommand matches. Auto mode is the built-in start for terminal and VS Code from v2.1.283, and it lets pushes to any branch of the working repo run without a prompt. Ask rules that match command content still prompt in auto mode. Conversation boundaries are re-read from the transcript, so compaction can lose them, and a deny rule is the hard guarantee. A PreToolUse allow or ask cannot override a deny or ask rule, and an exit-2 hook beats an allow rule.

### Project .claude/settings.json: permission rule syntax, precedence, merging with settings.local.json, trust

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs: settings, permissions, claude-directory, settings-reference)
- **Claim:** RULE SYNTAX: `Tool` or `Tool(specifier)`; bare `Bash` (= `Bash(*)`) matches all; examples: Bash(npm run build) exact, Bash(npm run *) prefix, Read(./.env), WebFetch(domain:example.com), Skill(name), Skill(name *), Agent(Explore), Agent(model:opus) / Agent(isolation:worktree) (deny/ask rules may match one top-level parameter via Tool(param:value); not for command/file_path/url), mcp__server and mcp__* tool globs (deny/ask), Workflow and Workflow(<name>). A bare-tool deny removes the tool from Claude's context; a scoped deny leaves the tool and blocks matching calls. Docs example: {"permissions":{"allow":["Bash(npm run *)","Bash(git commit *)"],"deny":["Bash(git push *)"]}}. Other permissions keys: ask, defaultMode (default | acceptEdits | plan | auto | dontAsk | bypassPermissions | manual), additionalDirectories, disableBypassPermissionsMode, disableAutoMode. defaultMode "auto" and "bypassPermissions" do NOT take effect from project or local settings (put them in ~/.claude/settings.json); for VS Code-started conversations only user, managed and --settings values are read. PRECEDENCE (highest first): managed > command line (--settings) > .claude/settings.local.json (personal, gitignored) > .claude/settings.json (shared, committed) > ~/.claude/settings.json. Scalars: highest level that sets the key wins. LISTS MERGE: permissions.allow/ask/deny and hooks are combined across files, not replaced (exceptions: fallbackModel, modelPicker, availableModels under managed, modelSettings). Deny from ANY scope beats allow from any other; a managed deny cannot be overridden by --allowedTools. TRUST: permissions.allow and additionalDirectories in a project's .claude/settings.json (repository-supplied) apply only after the workspace trust dialog is accepted for that folder (deny/ask apply regardless); claude -p / SDK never show the dialog. settings.local.json is applied without the trust step unless it is tracked in git (then it is treated as repo-supplied). Claude Code adds **/.claude/settings.local.json to the global git excludes when it saves a setting there. WHAT IS COMMITTED: CLAUDE.md, .claude/settings.json, .claude/agents/, .claude/skills/, .claude/workflows/ (shared on clone), .claude/output-styles/, .claude/rules/, .claude/hooks/ scripts, .claude/agent-memory/ (memory: project); NOT committed: .claude/settings.local.json, .claude/agent-memory-local/, .claude/worktrees/ (add to .gitignore). Add "$schema": "https://json.schemastore.org/claude-code-settings.json" for editor validation. `claude doctor` / /doctor validate settings files; /permissions lists every rule and its source file. `--settings '<json>'` overrides keys for one run and keeps the other keys from files.
- **Numbers:** 5 precedence levels; evaluation order deny > ask > allow
- **For the fleet:** Put fleet-wide rules (ask/deny for push, allow for the probe render command, worktree.baseRef, outputStyle, optionally agent) in committed .claude/settings.json; leave personal tweaks in settings.local.json so nothing owner-specific is committed. Because lists merge and deny wins everywhere, a deny for git push in the project file cannot be undone by a local allow. The owner must accept the trust dialog once after the first pull before project allow rules apply in the VS Code/CLI interactive sessions.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed on settings, permissions and settings-reference. Precedence is managed > --settings > settings.local.json > settings.json > ~/.claude/settings.json. Lists merge, and a deny in any scope beats an allow in any other. Project permissions.allow and additionalDirectories wait for the workspace trust dialog while deny and ask apply immediately. A settings.local.json tracked in git is treated as repo-supplied. Claude Code adds **/.claude/settings.local.json to the global git excludes on first write. defaultMode 'auto' and 'bypassPermissions' are ignored in project and local files. The VS Code extension never reads project files for the starting mode and reads claudeCode.initialPermissionMode from user settings only. The $schema URL is correct. The /permissions command and the full committed-versus-not file list were not each verified one by one.

### Headless runs: claude -p, --agents, --max-turns, --max-budget-usd, --output-format for unattended loops

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs: headless, cli-reference, agent-sdk/agent-loop, permission-modes)
- **Claim:** BASICS: `claude -p "prompt"` runs non-interactively and exits 0 on success, non-zero on failure (143 on SIGTERM). Without --bare, -p loads hooks, skills, subagents (.claude/agents), plugins, MCP servers, auto memory and CLAUDE.md like an interactive session, and runs project .claude/settings.json hooks even in a never-trusted folder; --bare skips all of that (Bash/Read/Edit only) and 'will become the default for -p in a future release', so then you must pass context explicitly: --agents <file-or-json>, --settings, --append-system-prompt(-file), --mcp-config, --plugin-dir. FLAGS (cli-reference): --agents '<json>' (keys = agent names; fields prompt + description, tools, disallowedTools, model, permissionMode, mcpServers, hooks, maxTurns, autoCompactWindow, skills, initialPrompt, memory, effort, background, omitClaudeMd, isolation; color/experimental ignored; with --print the value may be a path to a JSON file, v2.1.281+; validated at startup, v2.1.242+) - verbatim: claude --agents '{"reviewer":{"description":"Reviews code","prompt":"You are a code reviewer"}}' ; --agent <name> (main session as that agent; overrides settings agent); --max-turns N (print only; exits with an error when reached; counts tool-use turns; no limit by default); --max-budget-usd X (print only; client-side estimate; includes subagent spend; may overshoot by one response; spawning a subagent after the cap fails with 'Budget limit reached' and background subagents are stopped, v2.1.217+); --output-format text | json | stream-json (stream-json needs --verbose; add --include-partial-messages, --include-hook-events, --forward-subagent-text); --json-schema '<schema>' (result in structured_output); --model, --effort, --fallback-model sonnet,haiku; --permission-mode default|acceptEdits|plan|auto|dontAsk|bypassPermissions|manual; --allowedTools / --disallowedTools (rule syntax; trailing ' *' = prefix; space before * matters); --permission-prompts none (v2.1.259+: anything that would prompt is denied, Claude told not to retry, AskUserQuestion removed); --no-session-persistence; --resume/--continue; --settings; --setting-sources user,project,local; --add-dir; --worktree/-w works with -p (but no cleanup); --append-system-prompt(-file); --append-subagent-system-prompt(-file) (-p only; v2.1.205/v2.1.261+). /skill-name in the prompt expands user-invocable skills; /model, /effort, /config key=value, /output-style <name>, /advisor <model> work as arguments. RESULT: the json result has result, session_id, total_cost_usd, usage/modelUsage, num_turns, subtype (success | error_max_turns | error_max_budget_usd | error_during_execution | error_max_structured_output_retries), permission_denials in stream-json's final message; `result` exists only on success; all subtypes carry total_cost_usd, num_turns, session_id. A run waits for background subagents/workflows before exiting (idle cap CLAUDE_CODE_PRINT_BG_WAIT_CEILING_MS, default 10 min). WORKFLOWS/ULTRACODE IN -p: the ultracode keyword in a -p prompt does not start a workflow; the Workflow tool call goes through normal permission evaluation (allow rule Workflow or Workflow(<name>), auto/bypass mode, or hook allow). If settings contain "ultracode": true, override per run with --settings '{"ultracode":false}' (documented: false = sessions start with ultracode off). Subagent fork mode is off in -p. Bypass-permissions cannot run as root on Unix and the docs require a container/VM for fully unattended bypass.

MINIMAL OVERNIGHT ITERATION (composed from documented flags, NOT executed):
claude -p "/realism-iteration" --settings '{"ultracode":false}' --permission-mode acceptEdits --allowedTools "Read" "Edit" "Write" "Grep" "Glob" "Bash(./isaaclab.sh *)" "Bash(git status *)" "Bash(git diff *)" "Bash(git add *)" "Bash(git commit *)" --disallowedTools "Bash(git push *)" --permission-prompts none --max-turns 80 --max-budget-usd 10 --output-format json > runs/iter-$(date +%s).json
(then `jq -r '.subtype, .total_cost_usd' runs/iter-*.json`; start a fresh process per iteration; remove leftover worktrees with git worktree remove.)
- **Numbers:** exit 143 on SIGTERM; background-wait idle cap default 10 min; piped stdin cap 10MB; min versions: --permission-prompts v2.1.259, --agents file v2.1.281, budget enforcement v2.1.217
- **For the fleet:** Run the overnight loop as a shell loop of independent `claude -p` iterations, each bounded by --max-turns and --max-budget-usd, with an explicit allowlist, git push denied, --permission-prompts none (no hang on a prompt), JSON output logged and subtype checked. Pin the Claude Code version (or pass --agents/--settings explicitly) because --bare is slated to become the -p default and would stop .claude/agents and hooks from loading. Budget cap is a client-side estimate: leave headroom.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed on headless, cli-reference and agent-sdk/agent-loop. Exit code 143 on SIGTERM; --bare is recommended and 'will become the default for -p'; -p without --bare loads project hooks, skills and subagents even in an untrusted folder. --agents accepts JSON or a file path (v2.1.281+), is validated at startup (v2.1.242+), and ignores color and experimental. --max-turns counts tool-use turns only, exits with an error, and has no default limit. --max-budget-usd is a client-side estimate that can overshoot by one response, includes subagent spend, and gives 'Budget limit reached' (v2.1.217+). --permission-prompts none is v2.1.259+. --append-subagent-system-prompt(-file) are -p only (v2.1.205 and v2.1.261). The result subtypes are success, error_max_turns, error_max_budget_usd, error_during_execution and error_max_structured_output_retries; 'result' exists only on success; every subtype carries total_cost_usd, usage, num_turns and session_id. The 10 MB stdin cap and the 10-minute background-wait ceiling are confirmed. bypassPermissions refuses to run as root or under sudo on Linux/macOS, so the documented unattended path is a container as a non-root user. The overnight command line itself is composed, not a docs example, as the report says.

### Model aliases now valid, effort levels and the advisor tool (strongest model only where it matters)

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/advisor (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs: advisor, model-config, cli-reference)
- **Claim:** VALID ALIASES for --model, /model, subagent model, advisor: fable, opus, sonnet, haiku (plus best, default, opusplan, sonnet[1m], opus[1m]); full IDs e.g. claude-opus-5-5, claude-haiku-5-5, claude-fable-5-1, claude-sonnet-5. Ultracode is a Claude Code setting, not an effort level. ADVISOR TOOL (experimental, Anthropic API only - not Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry; needs feature-flag fetching, so it stays off if DISABLE_TELEMETRY-style variables are set): the main model consults a stronger server-side model at decision points (before committing to an approach, when stuck, before declaring done); the advisor receives the full conversation; advisor tokens are billed at the advisor model's rates. Enable: /advisor opus (also works in -p), settings {"advisorModel":"opus"}, or claude --advisor opus (flag not in --help). Accepted advisor must rank at or above the main model: Sonnet 5.5 main accepts Fable, Opus 5 or later, Sonnet 5.5; Haiku 5.5 main accepts Fable, Opus 4.7+, Sonnet 5+, Haiku 5.5; Haiku 4.5 can call but cannot be an advisor. Subagents inherit the configured advisor and the pairing check is applied against their own model. Documented pairings: Sonnet main + Opus advisor (Sonnet does routine work, escalates planning/ambiguous failures/completion checks); Haiku main + Opus advisor (lowest cost main). Toggling /advisor mid-session does not invalidate the main model's prompt cache; each advisor call reads the whole transcript uncached. Off: /advisor off or CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADVISOR_TOOL=1. Related: opusplan alias = opus while planning, sonnet while executing; --fallback-model chain (max 3, applies on overload/unavailable only, per-turn).
- **Numbers:** fallback chain max 3; advisor needs main model >= Haiku 4.5/Sonnet 4.6/Opus 4.6/Fable per page
- **For the fleet:** Cheapest way to get 'strong model only where it matters': run the lead and editors on sonnet, runners/log-readers on haiku, and add `advisorModel: opus` (or `claude --advisor opus` in the overnight command) so the strong model is consulted only at plan/verify moments. If the owner has Fable access, use it as advisor or for the single final judge, not for every agent. All of this is account-dependent, so first check `/model` and `/advisor` on the owner's PC.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed on https://code.claude.com/docs/en/advisor. It is experimental, Anthropic API only, and stays off if flag-fetching is disabled (e.g. DISABLE_TELEMETRY). Enable it with /advisor (works in -p, v2.1.260+), the advisorModel setting (accepted in any settings file), or --advisor (not shown in --help). The pairing table matches: Sonnet 5.5 main accepts Fable, Opus 5+ and Sonnet 5.5; Haiku 5.5 main accepts Fable, Opus 4.7+, Sonnet 5+ and Haiku 5.5; Haiku 4.5 can call but not act as an advisor. Subagents inherit the advisor and the pairing check uses their own model. Toggling it does not invalidate the main prompt cache. Disable it with /advisor off or CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADVISOR_TOOL=1. Haiku 5.5 as main or advisor needs v2.1.293+. Minor: the finding's design text puts the lead on Sonnet, but design rule 2 puts the lead on Opus. Pick one.

### VS Code extension vs CLI: what the fleet files do in each

- **Source:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/vs-code (Anthropic (Claude Code documentation), 2026-10-18 (page as fetched), Claude Code docs vs-code page; worktrees; workflows)
- **Claim:** SHARED: ~/.claude/settings.json is shared between extension and CLI (allowed commands, env, hooks, MCP); project .claude/ files (agents, skills, settings, output styles) are the same Claude Code engine. Output styles can be picked and created from the extension's '/' menu (v2.1.257 / v2.1.261+). Workflows: the docs say they work in the CLI, Desktop, IDE extensions, claude -p and the Agent SDK, and the extension shows an Ultracode switch under Effort when workflows are enabled and the model supports it. Subagents: the extension shows live progress rows (v2.1.269+), an agent count at the bottom of the prompt box, a tree of subagents with status/tokens, per-subagent read-only transcript, and a 'Run in background' button on a running subagent (v2.1.287+); /tasks works (v2.1.277+). Permission mode: the extension starts from claudeCode.initialPermissionMode (read from USER settings only), or ~/.claude/settings.json defaultMode (user-level), else the built-in default (auto since v2.1.283). Docs comparison table: slash commands/skills are a SUBSET in the extension (type / to see), MCP config yes, checkpoints yes, '!' bash shortcut no, tab completion no. For CLI-only features run `claude` in VS Code's integrated terminal (needs the standalone CLI install - the extension does not put claude on PATH) or set the extension setting useTerminal. --agent, --worktree, --tmux, --teammate-mode, --effort, -p are CLI flags; the docs for worktrees say to start worktree sessions per the worktrees page (CLI). Split-pane agent teams are not supported in VS Code's integrated terminal. NOT CONFIRMED in the pages read: that the extension honours the `agent` key in settings.json; that every /workflow-style command appears in the extension '/' menu.
- **Numbers:** VS Code 1.94.0+ required
- **For the fleet:** Everything the fleet needs (agents, skills, workflows, hooks, permissions, output style) is shared project config, so it works after git pull in both surfaces, but launch the orchestrator from the CLI (`claude --agent fleet-lead`, `claude -w`, `claude -p`) in VS Code's integrated terminal; use the extension panel for interactive review and its subagent tree. Verify once that /<workflow> and the `agent` setting appear in the extension before relying on them there.
- **Researcher says primary source confirmed:** true
- **Fact-checker:** source exists: true; claim supported: yes
- **Correction:** Confirmed on the vs-code, permission-modes and workflows pages. VS Code 1.94.0+; shared ~/.claude/settings.json and project .claude files. The Ultracode switch needs v2.1.284+. The output-styles menu is v2.1.257/v2.1.261. Subagent progress rows and the agent map need v2.1.269+; the 'Run in background' button needs v2.1.287; /tasks needs v2.1.277. The CLI comparison table shows commands as a subset, no '!' shortcut and no tab completion. The extension does not put 'claude' on PATH, and useTerminal exists. initialPermissionMode is read from user settings only and does not accept 'auto'. The extension start order is initialPermissionMode, then the last picked mode, then defaultMode from managed or ~/.claude/settings.json, then the built-in default. The report's own 'not confirmed' items (whether the extension honours the 'agent' setting, and whether saved workflows appear in its '/' menu) remain unconfirmed in the docs.

### Design rules proposed by the researcher

- Ship the fleet as committed project files and nothing else: .claude/agents/*.md, .claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md, .claude/workflows/*.js, .claude/output-styles/*.md, .claude/hooks/*.sh (executable bit committed), .claude/settings.json. Keep .claude/settings.local.json and .claude/worktrees/ out of git (add .claude/worktrees/ to .gitignore) and have the owner accept the workspace trust dialog once after the first pull, otherwise project permissions.allow rules do not apply. (Custom subagents: file locations and complete frontmatter; Skills: .claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md frontmatter, invocation, arguments, supporting files; Project .claude/settings.json: permission rule syntax, precedence, merging with settings.local.json, trust)
- Tier models per agent file, not globally: lead/final judge = opus (fable only if the owner's account has it), critic and code editors = sonnet, log readers and render runners = haiku; set effort per agent (low for runners). Do not set CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL_FORCE. For strong-model-at-decision-points add advisorModel: opus (or --advisor opus). Pin full model IDs in unattended runs. (Subagent model selection, aliases, effort and cost controls; Model aliases now valid, effort levels and the advisor tool (strongest model only where it matters))
- Make every agent prompt self-contained and give each a fixed output format (and a JSON schema when run inside a workflow), because subagents see no parent conversation and only their final text returns. Put the owner's explanation rules in each agent body too, since output styles do not reach non-fork subagents. (Custom subagents: file locations and complete frontmatter; Output styles (.claude/output-styles/*.md) for how the main assistant talks)
- Route by description: one trigger per agent, short descriptions (combined budget 15,000 tokens), 'use proactively' only on the critic (fires after a render) and nothing else; mark side-effecting skills (render, commit) disable-model-invocation: true so only the owner or the lead's explicit instruction starts a GPU render. (Automatic delegation, explicit invocation, nesting and Agent(...) allowlist; Skills: .claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md frontmatter, invocation, arguments, supporting files)
- Run the lead as the main session agent: .claude/agents/fleet-lead.md started with `claude --agent fleet-lead`, with tools: Agent(render-probe, realism-critic, material-editor, ...) as the roster allowlist; leaf agents omit Agent so they cannot fan out. Copy the essential owner rules into the lead body because its prompt replaces the default system prompt (CLAUDE.md still loads). (Running the main session as a custom agent (--agent / agent setting); Automatic delegation, explicit invocation, nesting and Agent(...) allowlist)
- Serialize the GPU by construction: exactly one agent (render-probe, haiku) is allowed to run ./isaaclab.sh; editor agents get no such permission (block it with an agent-frontmatter PreToolUse hook that exits 2) and never render; the loop script awaits renders one at a time and fans out only the edit stage. Optionally wrap the render command in flock via a PreToolUse hook updatedInput (our design on a documented mechanism; test on the owner PC). (Hooks: PreToolUse contract (stdin JSON, exit 2, permissionDecision ask/deny/allow), matchers and if; Dynamic workflows: saved scripts in .claude/workflows/ (publicly documented); Custom subagents: file locations and complete frontmatter)
- Parallel edits: give editor agents isolation: worktree, set {"worktree":{"baseRef":"head"}} so they branch from the owner's current HEAD (default branches from origin/<default>), list any untracked heavy directory the render needs in worktree.symlinkDirectories and copy .env-type files with .worktreeinclude, make each editor commit inside its worktree and report branch+commit, and let the lead merge only verified improvements. Note a subagent in a worktree reads CLAUDE.md from the main conversation, not its worktree. (Git worktrees: --worktree, subagent isolation: worktree, base branch and cleanup)
- Image critique: use a Read-equipped subagent (tools: Read, Grep, Glob; sonnet) that gets absolute PNG paths, a few frames per call, crops with ImageMagick when fine detail matters, and returns text only. Treat 'subagents can view PNGs' as inferred and verify with one test frame on the owner PC before depending on it. (Subagent image reading (Read tool on PNG frames); Subagent tool filters, foreground/background and behaviour under claude -p)
- Enforce owner rules with Claude Code mechanisms, not prose: permissions.ask [Bash(git push *)] plus deny for force pushes, plus a PreToolUse Bash hook (regex \bgit\b.*\bpush\b, permissionDecision ask) to catch git -C / -c forms; always exit 2 for hard blocks (exit 1 is non-blocking); add a CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE=true guard that blocks render commands. In unattended runs push stays denied. Auto mode is the default start mode, so CLAUDE.md alone does not stop a same-repo push. (Enforcing 'ask before git push': permissions.ask rule plus PreToolUse hook; Hooks: PreToolUse contract (stdin JSON, exit 2, permissionDecision ask/deny/allow), matchers and if)
- Do not use agent teams for this fleet: they are experimental, interactive-only, not worktree-isolated, token-heavy, and make named subagents turn into teammates when the env var is on. Keep CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS unset or 0 and use subagents plus a workflow script. (Agent teams (experimental): enable, display modes, limits, vs subagents)
- Encode the iteration loop (render -> critique -> parallel edits -> serial re-render -> keep verified) as a saved workflow committed in .claude/workflows/<name>.js: have Claude generate the first version with /workflow-authoring loaded (agent() option names for model, agent type and worktree isolation are not public), test on one small slice, save with s in /workflows, set workflowSizeGuideline to small while tuning, and allow it in unattended runs with permissions.allow Workflow(<name>). (Dynamic workflows: saved scripts in .claude/workflows/ (publicly documented); Headless runs: claude -p, --agents, --max-turns, --max-budget-usd, --output-format for unattended loops)
- Overnight loop = a shell loop of independent `claude -p` iterations, each with --max-turns, --max-budget-usd (leave headroom), --output-format json written to a log, an explicit --allowedTools list, --disallowedTools "Bash(git push *)", --permission-prompts none, --settings '{"ultracode":false}' (the owner's settings contain an ultracode key), and a post-run check of .subtype and .total_cost_usd; clean up leftover worktrees (git worktree remove) because -p never does. Do not use bypassPermissions on the owner's PC. Pin the Claude Code version or pass --agents/--settings explicitly because --bare is planned to become the -p default. (Headless runs: claude -p, --agents, --max-turns, --max-budget-usd, --output-format for unattended loops; Git worktrees: --worktree, subagent isolation: worktree, base branch and cleanup)
- Add quality gates with hooks: a SubagentStop/Stop hook can return decision block with a reason when the agent did not produce the required report (log line, frame paths, commit hash); mind the 8-consecutive-continuation cap and check stop_hook_active. (Hooks: PreToolUse contract (stdin JSON, exit 2, permissionDecision ask/deny/allow), matchers and if)
- Use VS Code for interactive review (subagent tree, output style picker) and the integrated-terminal CLI for orchestration (--agent, --worktree, -p); the project config is shared, but confirm once that saved workflows and the agent setting appear in the extension. Put auto/bypass defaultMode only in ~/.claude/settings.json because project/local files ignore them. (VS Code extension vs CLI: what the fleet files do in each; Project .claude/settings.json: permission rule syntax, precedence, merging with settings.local.json, trust)
- Persist critic learnings across iterations with memory: project on the critic (stored in .claude/agent-memory/<name>/MEMORY.md, committed, first 200 lines/25KB injected) or memory: local to keep it out of git; this only works while auto memory is on. (Custom subagents: file locations and complete frontmatter)

### Rules the fact-checker says to drop or fix

- Design rule 4 (route by description; mark side-effecting skills disable-model-invocation: true 'so only the owner or the lead's explicit instruction starts a GPU render'): FIX. Per the skills page, disable-model-invocation: true means only the user can invoke the skill. It also blocks Claude from calling it via the Skill tool, blocks preloading into subagents, and blocks scheduled-task firing. A lead agent therefore cannot launch such a skill. Either keep the skill model-invocable and gate the render with a hook/permission rule, or have the lead delegate the render to the render-probe subagent that runs the command through Bash. The user typing '/probe-render' or 'claude -p "/probe-render ..."' still works.
- Design rule 8 (image critique: tools Read, Grep, Glob, 'crops with ImageMagick when fine detail matters'): FIX. Cropping needs Bash, which the critic's tools list excludes, and ImageMagick on the owner's PC is unconfirmed. Either add Bash (read-only, hook-guarded) or drop the crop step. The 'subagents can view PNGs' premise is only inferred; the rule already says to verify once on the PC, so keep that step.
- Design rule 6, optional 'flock via PreToolUse updatedInput' wrapper: UNTESTED, and likely to need a fix. The docs say permission rules are evaluated against the input the hook returns. 'flock' is not in the documented stripped-wrapper list (timeout, time, nice, nohup, stdbuf, command, builtin, noglob, bare xargs). So a rewritten 'flock /tmp/gpu.lock ./isaaclab.sh ...' would not match an allow rule 'Bash(./isaaclab.sh *)' and would prompt, or be denied under -p with --permission-prompts none. This is my inference from the docs, not a docs statement. Fix: have the hook also return permissionDecision 'allow' (deny and ask rules still apply), or write the allow rule against the wrapped form. Test with 'claude --debug' on the PC.
- Design rule 12 (and Dynamic workflows finding): the premise 'the owner's settings contain an ultracode key' is unverifiable from the documentation. The remedy --settings '{"ultracode":false}' is documented ('false: sessions start with ultracode off'), so keep it, but do not assert the owner's setting. Likewise 'the /workflow-authoring skill is installed in the owner's environment' is an environment claim, not a docs fact.
- Design rule 2 (pin full model IDs in unattended runs): partly unverifiable. The docs print claude-opus-5-5, claude-haiku-5-5, claude-fable-5-1 and claude-sonnet-5 (Sonnet 5, not 5.5), but not claude-sonnet-5-5 in the part I read. This session's model ID is claude-sonnet-5-5, so use that for Sonnet 5.5 but label it docs-unconfirmed. Also the advisor finding puts the lead on sonnet while rule 2 puts the lead on opus. Resolve the contradiction, since both pairings are accepted by the advisor table. The effort-default statement in the model finding should say Opus 4.7 defaults to xhigh.
- Design rule 15 (memory: project on the critic): add a caveat. Setting memory auto-enables Read, Write and Edit for that subagent, so a critic declared with tools: Read, Grep, Glob gains write tools. The memory field has no effect if auto memory is off. Project-scope memory is committed to git.
- Design rule 10 (do not use agent teams): the conclusion is reasonable, but one stated reason ('not worktree-isolated') is inferred and not stated by the docs. Rely on the documented reasons instead: experimental and off by default, needs an interactive session, higher token cost, and named subagents become teammates when the env var is on.
- Design rule 5 / 'agent' setting: keep the --agent usage (confirmed). Drop or soften any claim that the 'agent' setting also sets the default for sessions dispatched from 'claude agents', since the sub-agents page does not state it. The note that VS Code honours the 'agent' key is also unconfirmed, as the report says.

### Open questions

- Workflow script API: the option names agent() accepts for per-agent model, subagent/agent type (to reuse .claude/agents definitions) and worktree isolation, and the exact parallel() signature, are not on the public workflows page (only schema, label, stallMs are shown). They are in the bundled /workflow-authoring skill, which was not read here (no code/skill execution allowed). Unconfirmed.
- Whether `claude -p "/<saved-workflow-name>"` runs a saved workflow directly in headless mode. Docs confirm saved workflows appear as /<name> commands and that skills expand via /name in -p, and that the Workflow tool can be allowed with a Workflow(<name>) rule, but a direct -p example for a workflow is not given. Unconfirmed.
- Whether a subagent's Read of a PNG works exactly like the main session (visual content in its own context) is inferred from the Read tool page plus the subagent tool inheritance rules; no page states it in one sentence. Also unconfirmed: image-reading quality of a haiku-class model for render artefacts.
- Whether the VS Code extension honours the `agent` key in .claude/settings.json and lists saved workflows in its '/' menu. The extension page says workflows work in IDE extensions and settings.json is shared, but does not mention the agent key. Unconfirmed.
- Whether the owner's account has Fable access (alias fable, and as advisor) and which aliases resolve to what on his plan: alias resolution is account/provider-dependent; run /model and /advisor on the PC. Also whether claude-sonnet-5-5 is the literal full ID for Sonnet 5.5 (the docs pages read print claude-sonnet-5, claude-opus-5-5, claude-haiku-5-5).
- The owner's ~/.claude/settings.json already has an 'ultracode' key (value not visible here). If it is true, interactive sessions plan a workflow for every substantive task; whether this also changes -p runs beyond the Workflow permission check is not stated (use --settings '{"ultracode":false}' to be deterministic).
- The exact default for cleanupPeriodDays (how long subagent worktrees with changes are kept before the sweep) was not read; it matters for disk use of many worktrees.
- Whether the repo's .venv / Isaac Sim symlink / large assets live inside the repo root (then worktree.symlinkDirectories is needed so a worktree can run ./isaaclab.sh) or outside it (~/.cache/drone_scene/... is outside). Not determinable from docs; check on the PC.
- The hook and settings snippets marked 'composed' (ask-on-push.sh, CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE guard, flock wrapper via updatedInput, the overnight command line, the example agent/skill/output-style files) use only documented fields but were NOT executed (cloud machine rules). Test each once on the owner's PC with `claude --debug` and /hooks before trusting them.
- The final ~11k chars of the sub-agents page, ~9.8k chars of the skills page, the last ~74k chars of the hooks reference and parts of the settings-reference page were not read; details only in those tails (e.g. WorktreeRemove input, a few settings entries) are not covered.

### Fact-checker summary

All 18 sources exist and open; every one is on code.claude.com/docs/en. I opened the sub-agents, model-config, workflows, agent-teams, worktrees, skills, output-styles, tools-reference, permissions, permission-modes, settings, settings-reference, cli-reference, headless, advisor, vs-code, hooks, hooks-guide, agent-sdk/agent-loop and agent-sdk/typescript pages. Fifteen findings are fully supported on every claim and number I could check. Three are partly supported: findings 1, 2 and 10, plus finding 5, whose 'subagents can view PNGs' conclusion the report already labels inferred. All problems are minor and listed per finding. None of the 18 findings is fabricated. The weaker parts are the design rules, not the sourced facts: rule 4 (a disable-model-invocation skill cannot be invoked by the lead), rule 8 (the critic cannot crop with ImageMagick without Bash), the untested flock/updatedInput wrapper in rule 6, the unverifiable claim about the owner's ultracode key, and the unconfirmed claude-sonnet-5-5 ID in rule 2. Limits of my check: the sub-agents page (111k characters), model-config (119k) and skills (110k) were read only through their first 100k characters.
